has a natural structure of a
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-vector space, with
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-structure given by
\mathscr{L}_{\psi}(\mathbb{F}_{q})=U_{\psi}(k)/U_{\psi+}(k)
. So, we have
\mathscr{L}_{\psi}=\mathscr{L}_{\psi}(\mathbb{F}_{q})\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
.

For
\alpha\in\Phi\cup\{0\}
,
\sigma\in[\mathcal{A}_{m}]
and
0<r\in\frac{1}{m}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, we define

\psi_{\alpha,\sigma,r}:=\text{ The smallest }\psi\in\Psi_{m}(\mathcal{A})\text{ with }\nabla\psi=\alpha\text{ such that }\psi(x)\geq r\>\forall\>x\in\sigma

\psi_{\alpha,\sigma,r+}:=\text{ The smallest }\psi\in\Psi_{m}(\mathcal{A})\text{ with }\nabla\psi=\alpha\text{ such that }\psi(x)>r\>\forall\>x\in\sigma

Let
S
be a
k
-split maximal torus such that
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{A}(S,k)
(cf. Corollary 7.6.9,
[
KP23
]
) and let
\mathfrak{s}=\mathrm{Lie}(S)
. Then, from Proposition 13.2.5 in
[
KP23
]
, we have

G_{\sigma,r}=S(k)_{r}\times\prod_{\alpha\in\Phi}U_{\psi_{\alpha,\sigma,r}}(k)

and

G_{\sigma,r+}=S(k)_{r+}\times\prod_{\alpha\in\Phi}U_{\psi_{\alpha,\sigma,r+}}(k)

Note that for
\alpha\not\in\Phi_{\sigma}
,
\psi_{\alpha,\sigma,r}=\psi_{\alpha,\sigma,r+}
, and hence we have

G_{\sigma,r}/G_{\sigma,r+}=\left(S(k)_{r}/S(k)_{r+}\right)\times\prod_{\alpha\in\Phi_{\sigma}}U_{\psi_{\alpha,\sigma,r}}(k)/U_{\psi_{\alpha,\sigma,r}+}(k)

(4.1.4)

Using the above-mentioned ideas, we have the following proposition about the structure of the Moy-Prasad filtration quotient
G_{\sigma,r}/G_{\sigma,r+}
, which is similar to Proposition 2.5.4,
[
MS20
]
.

Proposition 4.2
.

Let
\sigma\in[\mathcal{B}_{m}]
be a refined facet, and
\tau,\;\tau^{\prime}\in[\mathcal{B}_{m}]
be two refined facets which are
\sigma
-opposite. Then, we have (Iwahori decomposition):

G_{\sigma,r}/G_{\sigma,r+}=G_{\tau,r}/G_{\tau,r+}\oplus G_{\tau,r+}/G_{\sigma,r+}\oplus G_{\tau^{\prime},r+}/G_{\sigma,r+}

(4.1.5)

Further, we have a similar decomposition for the Moy-Prasad quotients of the Lie algebra

\mathfrak{g}_{\sigma,r}/\mathfrak{g}_{\sigma,r+}=\mathfrak{g}_{\tau,r}/\mathfrak{g}_{\tau,r+}\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{\tau,r+}/\mathfrak{g}_{\sigma,r+}\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{\tau^{\prime},r+}/\mathfrak{g}_{\sigma,r+}

(4.1.6)

and the Moy-Prasad isomorphism (Theorem 13.5.1,
[
KP23
]
)

G_{\sigma,r}/G_{\sigma,r+}\cong\mathfrak{g}_{\sigma,r}/\mathfrak{g}_{\sigma,r+}

(4.1.7)

induces isomorphisms

G_{\tau,r+}/G_{\sigma,r+}\cong\mathfrak{g}_{\tau,r+}/\mathfrak{g}_{\sigma,r+}\>\>\text{ and }\>\>G_{\tau^{\prime},r+}/G_{\sigma,r+}\cong\mathfrak{g}_{\tau^{\prime},r+}/\mathfrak{g}_{\sigma,r+}

(4.1.8)

Proof.

From (
4.1.4
), we see that

G_{\sigma,r}/G_{\sigma,r+}=\left(S(k)_{r}/S(k)_{r+}\right)\times\prod_{\alpha\in\Phi_{\sigma}}U_{\psi_{\alpha,\sigma,r}}(k)/U_{\psi_{\alpha,\sigma,r}+}(k).

For
\alpha\in\Phi_{\tau}
, we have
\psi_{\alpha,\sigma,r}=\psi_{\alpha,\tau,r}
and
\psi_{\alpha,\sigma,r+}=\psi_{\alpha,\tau,r+}
. Further, for
\alpha\in\Phi_{\sigma,\tau}
,
\psi_{\alpha,\sigma,r}=\psi_{\alpha,\tau,r+}
and for
\alpha\in\Phi_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime}}
,
\psi_{\alpha,\sigma,r}=\psi_{\alpha,\tau^{\prime},r+}
. This gives (
4.1.5
). Further, from the proof of Theorem 13.5.1,
[
KP23
]
, we note that there exist isomorphisms
U_{\psi_{\alpha,\sigma,r}}(k)/U_{\psi_{\alpha,\sigma,r}+}(k)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathfrak{u}_{\psi_{\alpha,\sigma,r}}(k)/\mathfrak{u}_{\psi_{\alpha,\sigma,r}+}(k)
and
S(k)_{r}/S(k)_{r+}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathfrak{s}(k)_{r}/\mathfrak{s}(k)_{r+}
. This gives the triangular decomposition in (
4.1.6
) and the isomorphisms in (
4.1.8
).
∎

Note that each component of the direct sum in (
4.1.5
) is a
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-vector space. We have a similar decomposition of
\kappa_{K}=\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-vector spaces

G(K)_{\sigma,r}/G(K)_{\sigma,r+}=G(K)_{\tau,r}/G(K)_{\tau,r+}\oplus G(K)_{\tau,r+}/G(K)_{\sigma,r+}\oplus G(K)_{\tau^{\prime},r+}/G(K)_{\sigma,r+}

(4.1.9)

Further each of the isomorphisms (
4.1.7
), (
4.1.8
) are compatible with unramified algebraic extensions of
k
, and hence holds over
K
as isomorphisms of
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-spaces. Let
\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}
denote the
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-vector space
G(K)_{\tau,r+}/G(K)_{\sigma,r+}\cong\mathfrak{g}(K)_{\tau,r+}/\mathfrak{g}(K)_{\sigma,r+}
, and
\bar{\mathfrak{p}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}
denote the
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-vector space
G(K)_{\tau,r}/G(K)_{\sigma,r+}\cong\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}
. The
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-vector space
G_{\tau,r+}/G_{\sigma,r+}
gives
\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}
an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-structure, and hence we can denote it by
\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}=\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
. Similar statements are also true for
\bar{\mathfrak{p}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}
and the other components of the decomposition in (
4.1.9
), and their corresponding counterparts in (
4.1.5
).

4.2
Fourier Transform on positive depth Moy-Prasad quotients

Let
\mathfrak{g}=\text{Lie}(G)
and
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
be its dual. Given
x\in\mathcal{B}(G)
, we have the Moy-Prasad filtrations for
\mathfrak{g}(k)
denoted by
\mathfrak{g}_{x,r}:=\mathfrak{g}(k)_{x,r}
, and that of
\mathfrak{g}^{*}(k)
denoted by
\mathfrak{g}^{*}_{x,-r}:=\mathfrak{g}^{*}(k)_{x,-r}
. We can define
\mathfrak{g}_{\sigma,r}
and
\mathfrak{g}^{*}_{\sigma,-r}
for
\sigma\in[\mathcal{B}_{m}]
,
r\in\frac{1}{m}\mathbb{Z}
similarly to
G_{\sigma,r}
. The
\mathfrak{O}_{k}
-module
\mathfrak{g}_{\sigma,r}
is stable under the adjoint action of
G_{\sigma,r}
, and the action of
G_{\sigma,r+}
on
\mathfrak{g}_{\sigma,r}/\mathfrak{g}_{\sigma,r+}
is trivial, which induces an action of
\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}
on
\mathfrak{g}_{\sigma,r}/\mathfrak{g}_{\sigma,r+}
. Similarly, we have an action of
\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}
on
\mathfrak{g}^{*}_{\sigma,-r}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}_{\sigma,-r+}
induced by the co-adjoint action. For
r>0
, the Moy-Prasad isomorphism gives a
\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}
-equivariant isomorphism

G_{\sigma,r}/G_{\sigma,r+}\cong\mathfrak{g}_{\sigma,r}/\mathfrak{g}_{\sigma,r+}.

(4.2.1)

Further, for
r>0
, the
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-bilinear map

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}^{*}_{\sigma,-r}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}_{\sigma,-r+}\times\mathfrak{g}_{\sigma,r}/\mathfrak{g}_{\sigma,r+}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbb{F}_{q}

\displaystyle(X,Y)

\displaystyle\longmapsto X(Y)\text{ mod }\varpi\mathfrak{O}_{k}

is a non-degenerate
\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}
-invariant pairing, and gives a
\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}
-equivariant isomorphism between
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}\cong(\mathfrak{g}_{\sigma,r}/\mathfrak{g}_{\sigma,r+})^{*}
and
\mathfrak{g}^{*}_{\sigma,-r}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}_{\sigma,-r+}
.

We develop a theory of Fourier transforms on positive depth Moy-Prasad filtration quotients, mostly following the ideas in
[
Let96
]
which studies the space of adjoint-invariant functions finite Lie algebras. Our theory is a generalisation, and would reduce to the statements in Section 4 of
[
Let96
]
if we consider integral depths and use a non-degenerate invariant bilinear form to identify the Lie algebra and its dual. The Fourier transorm in the afore-mentioned paper was used in
[
CB24
]
to study stable functions on integral depth quotients.

Let
C(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})
denote the space of
\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}
-invariant functions on
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}
equipped with the convolution product

f*f^{\prime}(X)=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right|^{-1/2}\sum_{Y\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}f(X-Y)f^{\prime}(Y).

(4.2.2)

and
C\left((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}\right)
denote the space of
\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}
-invariant functions on
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}
equipped with point-wise multiplication. Note that we can also have a similar convolution product on
C\left((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}\right)
. For
f,g\in C(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})
, we can define a positive definite non-singular Hermitian form
(\>,\>)
on
C(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})
by

(f,g)=\left|\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}\right|^{-1}\sum_{Y\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}f(Y)\overline{g(Y)}

We can similarly define a positive definite non-singular Hermitian form on
C\left((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}\right)
.

We fix a non-trivial additive character
\tilde{\psi}:\mathbb{F}_{q}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. For a finite dimensional vector space
V
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, the choice of a non-trivial character
\tilde{\psi}
determines an identification of the dual space
V^{*}
and the Pontryagin dual
\widehat{V}
.
Denote by
\mathbb{C}[V]
(resp.
\mathbb{C}[V^{*}]
) the space of complex valued functions on
V
(resp.
V^{*}
). We have the Fourier-transform

\mathcal{F}_{V}:\mathbb{C}[V]\to\mathbb{C}[V^{*}]

defined by the formula

\mathcal{F}_{V}(f)(X^{*})=|V|^{-1/2}\sum_{Y\in V}\psi((X^{*}(Y))f(Y).

(4.2.3)

We can define a Fourier Transform
\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}:C(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})\longrightarrow C\left((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}\right)
following the idea in (
4.2.3
), given by the formula :

\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f)(X^{*})=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right|^{-1/2}\sum_{Y\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}\tilde{\psi}(X^{*}(Y))f(Y).

(4.2.4)

and a Fourier Transform
\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}:C\left((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}\right)\longrightarrow C(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})
given by

\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}(f)(Y)=\left|(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}\right|^{-1/2}\sum_{X^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\tilde{\psi}(X^{*}(Y))f(X^{*}).

(4.2.5)

Let
\sigma\preceq\tau
,
\sigma\preceq\tau^{\prime}
such that
\tau
and
\tau^{\prime}
are
\sigma
-opposite. Then from
(
4.1.5
)
we know

\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\cong\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F}

(4.2.6)

as
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-vector spaces. The projection maps to the subspaces of the Iwahori decomposition give a natural identification of
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})^{*}
,
(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F})^{*}
and
(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F})^{*}
as subspaces of
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}
and we have

(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}\cong(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F})^{*}\oplus(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})^{*}\oplus(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F})^{*}

For
\sigma\preceq\tau
, we have the normalized parabolic restriction map
\text{Res}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}:C(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})\rightarrow C(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})
defined by

\text{Res}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}(f)(X)=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}\right|^{-1}\sum_{N\in\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}}f(X+N)

(4.2.7)

and the restriction map for the dual case
\text{Res}^{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r})^{*}}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r})^{*}}:C\left((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}\right)\rightarrow C\left((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})^{*}\right)
defined by

\mathrm{Res}^{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r})^{*}}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r})^{*}}(\tilde{f})(X^{*})=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F}\right|^{-1}\sum_{N^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F})^{*}}\tilde{f}(X^{*}+N^{*}).

(4.2.8)

For
\sigma\preceq\tau_{1}\preceq\tau_{2}
, we have transitivity of restriction maps

\text{Res}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau_{2},r}}=\text{Res}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau_{1},r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau_{2},r}}\circ\text{Res}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau_{1},r}}\>\>\text{ and }\>\>\text{Res}^{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r})^{*}}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau_{2},r})^{*}}=\text{Res}^{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau_{1},r})^{*}}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau_{2},r})^{*}}\circ\text{Res}^{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r})^{*}}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau_{1},r})^{*}}

We give some details of the dual case. Let
\tau_{1},\tau_{1}^{\prime}
and
\tau_{2},\tau_{2}^{\prime}
be
\sigma
-opposite pairs, and
\tau_{2},\tau_{2}^{\prime\prime}
be
\tau_{1}
-opposite. This implies
\tau_{1}^{\prime}\preceq\tau_{2}^{\prime}
and
\sigma\preceq\tau_{2}^{\prime\prime}
. Using the Iwahori decomposition as in (
4.2.6
) and the fact that
\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau_{2}^{\prime},r}^{F}\cong\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau_{1}^{\prime},r}^{F}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\tau_{1}^{\prime},\tau_{2}^{\prime},r}^{F}
, the transitivity of restriction in the dual case would follow if
\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\tau_{1}^{\prime},\tau_{2}^{\prime},r}^{F}\cong\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}^{\prime\prime},r}^{F}
and hence their duals are isomorphic. We have

\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\tau_{1}^{\prime},\tau_{2}^{\prime},r}^{F}=G_{\tau_{2}^{\prime},r+}/{G_{\tau_{1}^{\prime},r+}}\cong\prod_{\alpha\in\Phi_{\tau_{1}^{\prime},\tau_{2}^{\prime}}}U_{\psi_{\alpha,\tau_{2}^{\prime},r+}}(k)/{U_{\psi_{\alpha,\tau_{1}^{\prime},r+}}}(k)\cong\prod_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\Phi_{\tau_{1}^{\prime},\tau_{2}^{\prime}}\\
r-\alpha(\tau_{1}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}}\mathscr{L}_{\psi_{\alpha,\tau_{2}^{\prime},r+}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})

and

\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}^{\prime\prime},r}^{F}=G_{\tau_{2}^{\prime\prime},r+}/{G_{\tau_{1},r+}}\cong\prod_{\alpha\in\Phi_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}^{\prime\prime}}}U_{\psi_{\alpha,\tau_{2}^{\prime\prime},r+}}(k)/{U_{\psi_{\alpha,\tau_{1},r+}}}(k)\cong\prod_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\Phi_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}^{\prime\prime}}\\
r-\alpha(\tau_{1})\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}}\mathscr{L}_{\psi_{\alpha,\tau_{2}^{\prime\prime},r+}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})

We certainly have
\Phi_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}^{\prime\prime}}=\Phi_{\tau_{1}^{\prime},\tau_{2}^{\prime}}
just from their definitions, and since
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{1}^{\prime}
are
\sigma
-opposite,
r-\alpha(\tau_{1}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}\Longleftrightarrow r-\alpha(\tau_{1})\in\mathbb{Z}
. Further, for
\alpha\in\Phi_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}^{\prime\prime}}
, we have
\psi_{\alpha,\tau_{2}^{\prime\prime},r+}=\psi_{\alpha,\tau_{2}^{\prime},r+}
and hence
\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\tau_{1}^{\prime},\tau_{2}^{\prime},r}^{F}\cong\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}^{\prime\prime},r}^{F}
.

Then, for
\tilde{f}\in C\left((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}\right)
and
X^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau_{2},r}^{F})^{*}

\displaystyle\text{Res}^{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r})^{*}}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau_{2},r})^{*}}(\tilde{f})(X^{*})

\displaystyle=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau_{2}^{\prime},r}^{F}\right|^{-1}\tilde{f}(X^{*}+N^{*})

\displaystyle=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau_{1}^{\prime},r}^{F}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\tau_{1}^{\prime},\tau_{2}^{\prime},r}^{F}\right|^{-1}\sum_{N^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau_{1}^{\prime},r}^{F})^{*}\oplus(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\tau_{1}^{\prime},\tau_{2}^{\prime},r}^{F})^{*}}\tilde{f}(X^{*}+N^{*})

\displaystyle=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau_{1}^{\prime},r}^{F}\right|^{-1}\sum_{N_{1}^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau_{1}^{\prime},r}^{F})^{*}}\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}^{\prime\prime},r}^{F}\right|^{-1}\sum_{N_{2}^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}^{\prime\prime},r}^{F})^{*}}\tilde{f}(X^{*}+N_{1}^{*}+N_{2}^{*})

\displaystyle=\text{Res}^{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r})^{*}}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau_{1},r})^{*}}\circ\text{Res}^{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau_{1},r})^{*}}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau_{2},r})^{*}}(\tilde{f})(X^{*})

We can also define an induction map, similar to the Harish-Chandra induction in the Lie algebra case.

\mathrm{Ind}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}f(X)=\left|\mathbb{G}_{\tau}^{F}\right|^{-1}\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}\right|^{-1}\sum_{{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}\\
\prescript{g}{}{}X\in\bar{\mathfrak{p}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}\end{subarray}}}}f^{\vee}(\prescript{g}{}{}X)

where
f^{\vee}(X+N)=f(X)
where
X\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}^{F}_{\tau,r}
and
N\in\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}
.

Lemma 4.3
.

The maps
\mathrm{Res}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}
and
\mathrm{Ind}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}
are adjoint with respect to the inner products
(\>,\>)_{{}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}}
and
(\>,\>)_{{}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}}}
on
C(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})
and
C(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})
respectively.

Proof.

Let
f\in C(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})
and
g\in C(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})
. Then,

\displaystyle(f,\mathrm{Ind}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}(g))

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}\right|^{-1}\sum_{Y\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}f(Y)\overline{\mathrm{Ind}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}(g)}

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}\right|^{-1}\left|\mathbb{G}_{\tau}^{F}\right|^{-1}\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}\right|^{-1}\sum_{Y\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}f(Y)\sum_{{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}\\
\prescript{h}{}{}Y\in\bar{\mathfrak{p}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}\end{subarray}}}}\overline{g^{\vee}(\prescript{h}{}{}Y)}

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}\right|^{-1}\left|\mathbb{G}_{\tau}^{F}\right|^{-1}\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}\right|^{-1}\sum_{h\in\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}}\sum_{Y\in\mathrm{Ad}(h^{-1})\bar{\mathfrak{p}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}}f(\prescript{h}{}{}Y)\overline{g^{\vee}(\prescript{h}{}{}Y)}

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}\right|^{-1}\left|\mathbb{G}_{\tau}^{F}\right|^{-1}\sum_{h\in\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}}\sum_{Y\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}}\overline{g(Y)}\left(\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}\right|^{-1}\sum_{N\in\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}}f(Y+N)\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\mathrm{Res}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}(f),g\right)

∎

Similar definition for
\mathrm{Ind}^{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r})^{*}}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r})^{*}}
can be made in the dual case as well, with similar properties.

Proposition 4.4

(Properties of Fourier transform)
.

Let
f,g\in C\left(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right)
and
\tilde{f},\tilde{g}\in C\left((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}\right)
. The Fourier transforms defined in (
4.2.4
) and (
4.2.5
) have the following properties:

(i)

\left(\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f),\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(g)\right)_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}=\left(f,g\right)_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}
.

(ii)

\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}(\tilde{f})=(\tilde{f})^{-}
and
\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f)=f^{-}
, where
f^{-}(X)=f(-X)
.

(iii)

\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f*g)=\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f)\cdot\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(g)
and
\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}(\tilde{f}*\tilde{g})=\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}(\tilde{f})\cdot\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}(\tilde{g})
.

(iv)

\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f\cdot g)=\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f)*\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(g)
and
\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}(\tilde{f}\cdot\tilde{g})=\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}(\tilde{f})*\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}(\tilde{g})
.

As a consequence, we see that the Fourier transform
\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}:C(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})\longrightarrow C\left((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}\right)
is an algebra isomorphism with inverse given by
\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}
, where multiplication on
C\left((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}\right)
is given by the usual pointwise one. The analogous result holds true for
\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}:C\left((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}\right)\longrightarrow C(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})
.

Proof.

Most of these proofs follow from simple calculations using the definitions.

(i)

Follows immediately from calculations.

(ii)

Let
Y\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f)(Y)

\displaystyle=\left|(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}\right|^{-1/2}\sum_{X^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\tilde{\psi}(X^{*}(Y))\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f)(X^{*})

\displaystyle=\left|(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}\right|^{-1/2}\sum_{X^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\tilde{\psi}(X^{*}(Y))\left(\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right|^{-1/2}\sum_{Y_{1}\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}\tilde{\psi}(X^{*}(Y_{1}))f(Y_{1})\right)

\displaystyle=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right|^{-1}\sum_{X^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\sum_{Y_{1}\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}\tilde{\psi}(X^{*}(Y+Y_{1}))f(Y_{1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{Y_{1}\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}f(Y_{1})\left(\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right|^{-1}\sum_{X^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\tilde{\psi}(X^{*}(Y+Y_{1}))\right)

We know that the inside sum
\frac{1}{\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right|}\sum_{X^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\tilde{\psi}(X^{*}(Y+Y_{1}))=\begin{cases}0,&\text{ if }Y\neq-Y_{1}\\
1,&\text{ if }Y=-Y_{1}\\
\end{cases}
which gives us
\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f)(Y)=f(-Y)
and we are done.

(iii)

Let
X^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f*g)(X^{*})

\displaystyle=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right|^{-1/2}\sum_{Y\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}\tilde{\psi}(X^{*}(Y))f*g(Y)

\displaystyle=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right|^{-1/2}\sum_{Y\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}\tilde{\psi}(X^{*}(Y))\left(\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right|^{-1/2}\sum_{Z\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}f(Y-Z)g(Z)\right)

\displaystyle=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right|^{-1}\sum_{Y\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}\tilde{\psi}(X^{*}(Y-Z))f(Y-Z)\sum_{Z\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}\tilde{\psi}(X^{*}(Z))g(Z)

\displaystyle=\left(\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right|^{-1/2}\sum_{Y\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}\tilde{\psi}(X^{*}(Y-Z))f(Y-Z)\right)\left(\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right|^{-1/2}\sum_{Z\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}\tilde{\psi}(X^{*}(Z))g(Z)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f)(X^{*})\cdot\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(g)(X^{*})

(iv)

For any
f,g\in C\left(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right)
, we see using
(i)
that

\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f*g)=\left(\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f)\right)*\left(\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(g)\right).

(4.2.9)

Further, applying
\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}
to both sides of
(ii)
, we have

\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f*g)=f*g=\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\left(\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f)\cdot\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(g)\right)

Then, applying
\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}
to both sides, we have by using (
4.2.9
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f*g)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\left(\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f)\cdot\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(g)\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f)\right)*\left(\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(g)\right).

For any
\tilde{f},\tilde{g}\in C\left((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}\right)
, we can choose
f,g\in C\left(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right)
such that
\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f)=\tilde{f}
and
\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(g)=\tilde{g}
(follows from
(i)
). Then, using the previous equation, we have
\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}(\tilde{f}\cdot\tilde{g})=\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}(\tilde{f})*\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}(\tilde{g})
and we are done.

We have given the proofs for one of the results in each of
(ii)
,
(iii)
and
(iv)
. The others follow from exactly similar calculations. Once we have these, the last statement follows immediately.
∎

Proposition 4.5

(Compatibility with restriction maps)
.

Let
\sigma,\tau\in[\mathcal{B}_{m}]
such that
\sigma\preceq\tau
and
\tau^{\prime},\;\tau
be
\sigma
-opposite. We have

(i)

\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F}\right|^{1/2}\mathrm{Res}^{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r})^{*}}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r})^{*}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}\right|^{1/2}\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}}\circ\mathrm{Res}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}
.

(ii)

\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F}\right|^{1/2}\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})^{*}}\circ\mathrm{Res}^{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r})^{*}}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r})^{*}}=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}\right|^{1/2}\mathrm{Res}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}
.

Proof.

Let
f\in C\left(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right)
and
X^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})^{*}
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Res}^{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r})^{*}}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r})^{*}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f)(X^{*})

\displaystyle=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F}\right|^{-1}\sum_{N^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F})^{*}}\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f)(X^{*}+N^{*})

\displaystyle=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F}\right|^{-1}\sum_{N^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F})^{*}}\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right|^{-1/2}\sum_{Y\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}\tilde{\psi}((X^{*}+N^{*})(Y))f(Y)

\displaystyle=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right|^{-1/2}\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F}\right|^{-1}\sum_{Y\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}\tilde{\psi}(X^{*}(Y))f(Y)\sum_{N^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F})^{*}}\tilde{\psi}(N^{*}(Y)).

Now,
\sum_{N^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F})^{*}}\tilde{\psi}(N^{*}(Y))=0
if
N^{*}(Y)\neq 0
since
N^{*}\mapsto\tilde{\psi}(N^{*}(Y))
is a character of
(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F})^{*}
. Since
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\cong\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F}
, the sum is only non-zero when
N^{*}(Y)=0
, i.e.,
Y\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}
. So, it follows that

\sum_{N^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F})^{*}}\tilde{\psi}(N^{*}(Y))=\begin{cases}0,&\text{ when }Y\in\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F}\\
\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F}\right|,&\text{ when }Y\not\in\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F}\end{cases}

Using the above, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Res}^{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r})^{*}}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r})^{*}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f)(X^{*})

\displaystyle=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right|^{-1/2}\sum_{Y\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}}\tilde{\psi}(X^{*}(Y))f(Y)

\displaystyle=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right|^{-1/2}\sum_{Y_{1}\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}}\tilde{\psi}(X^{*}(Y_{1}))\sum_{N\in\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}}f(Y_{1}+N)

\displaystyle=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right|^{-1/2}\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}\right|\sum_{Y_{1}\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}}\tilde{\psi}(X^{*}(Y_{1}))\mathrm{Res}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}(f)(Y_{1})

\displaystyle=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}\right|^{1/2}\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F}\right|^{-1/2}\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}}\circ\mathrm{Res}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}(f)(X^{*})

∎

Remark 4.6
.

Let us define
c_{\sigma,\tau,r}=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}\right|^{1/2}\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F}\right|^{-1/2}
for
\sigma\preceq\tau,\tau^{\prime}
and
\tau
,
\tau^{\prime}
being
\sigma
-opposite. Then we can restate the results in Proposition
4.5
as follows:

(i)

\mathrm{Res}^{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r})^{*}}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r})^{*}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}=c_{\sigma,\tau,r}\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}}\circ\mathrm{Res}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}
.

(ii)

\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})^{*}}\circ\mathrm{Res}^{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r})^{*}}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r})^{*}}=c_{\sigma,\tau,r}\mathrm{Res}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}
.

Note that, for
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
,
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}\cong\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma}
and the Iwahori decomposition of
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}
for agrees with the usual triangular decomposition of the Lie algebra
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma}
. In this case, the theory of Fourier transform developed here reduces to the case studied in
[
Let96
]
. Further, for integral depths,
c_{\sigma,\tau,r}=1
and the restriction and Fourier transform maps commute, as expected.

Proposition 4.7

(Compatibility with induction)
.

Let
\sigma,\tau\in[\mathcal{B}_{m}]
such that
\sigma\preceq\tau
and
\tau^{\prime},\;\tau
be
\sigma
-opposite. We have

(i)

c_{\sigma,\tau,r}\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}\circ\mathrm{Ind}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}=\mathrm{Ind}^{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r})^{*}}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r})^{*}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}}
.

(ii)

c_{\sigma,\tau,r}\mathrm{Ind}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})^{*}}=\mathrm{Ind}^{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r})^{*}}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r})^{*}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}}

Proof.

Let
f\in C(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})
and
g\in C((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*})
. Then,

\displaystyle(\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}\circ\mathrm{Ind}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}(f),g)

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{Ind}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}(f),\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}^{-1}(g))

\displaystyle=(f,\mathrm{Res}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}^{-1}(g))

\displaystyle=(f,c_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{-1}\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}}^{-1}\circ\mathrm{Res}^{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r})^{*}}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r})^{*}}(g))

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}}(f),c_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{-1}\mathrm{Res}^{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r})^{*}}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r})^{*}}(g))

\displaystyle=(c_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{-1}\mathrm{Ind}^{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r})^{*}}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r})^{*}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}}(f),g)

Since, the inner product is non-degenerate and
f
and
g
were arbitrary this proves the first statement, and the second one follows similarly.
∎

4.3
Stable functions

We define and study the properties of stable functions on Moy-Prasad quotients, extending ideas developed in
[
CB24
]
using the theory of Fourier transforms developed in the previous section. Throughout,
r\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, and we will consider only
m
such that
p\nmid m
. Let
\Gamma_{k}
denote
\Gal(\bar{k}/k)
, with Krull topology. We fix an uniformizer
\varpi\in\mathfrak{O}_{k}
and an arbitrary
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
be such that
p\nmid m
. Let
E
be the unique tamely totally ramified extension of
K
of degree
m
. We further fix
\gamma\in\mathfrak{O}_{E}
such that
\gamma^{m}=\varpi
. Then
k(\gamma)=E^{\prime}
is a tame totally ramified extension of
k
, and
E=(E^{\prime})^{u}=K(\gamma)
. Let
\mathfrak{F}_{\gamma}
denote the unique Frobenius element in
\Gal(E/k)
such that
\mathfrak{F}_{\gamma}(\gamma)=\gamma
. The subgroup
\langle\mathfrak{F}_{\gamma}\rangle\subset\Gal(E/k)
lies inside
\Gal(E/E^{\prime})
, and
\mathfrak{F}_{\gamma}
is the topological generator of
\Gal(E/E^{\prime})
.
\Gal(E/K)
is a cyclic group of order
m
, generated by
\vartheta
(say). Further, consider the natural projection map
\Gal(E/k)\longrightarrow\Gal(K/k)
and we denote the image of
\mathfrak{F}_{\gamma}
by
\mathfrak{F}
, which is the topological generator of
\Gamma^{ur}:=\Gal(K/k)
.

We go back to the setting of the fixed
k
-split maximal torus
T
, and the apartment
\mathscr{A}=\mathcal{A}_{T}
corresponding to it. Let
k^{t}
be the maximal tamely ramified extension of
k
, and
\mathbb{T}
be the
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
- group defined by
\mathbb{T}(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q})=T(k^{t})_{0}/T(k^{t})_{0+}
.
\mathbb{T}
is the reductive quotient of the special fiber of the connected Neron model of
T
. Since
T
is
k
-split we have
\mathbb{T}(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q})=T(K)_{0}/T(K)_{0+}=T(E)_{0}/T(E)_{0+}
. Further,
\mathbb{T}
is
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-split and has a
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-structure with the group of
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-rational points given by
\mathbb{T}(\mathbb{F}_{q})=T(k)_{0}/T(k)_{0+}=T(E^{\prime})_{0}/T(E^{\prime})_{0+}
. Let
\bar{\mathfrak{t}}=\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbb{T})
and
\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}
be its dual.Then,
\bar{\mathfrak{t}}(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q})=\mathfrak{t}(K)_{0}/\mathfrak{t}(K)_{0+}=\mathfrak{t}(E)_{0}/\mathfrak{t}(E)_{0+}
and has a
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-structure given by
\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{F}=\bar{\mathfrak{t}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})=\mathfrak{t}(k)_{0}/\mathfrak{t}(k)_{0+}=\mathfrak{t}(E^{\prime})_{0}/\mathfrak{t}(E^{\prime})_{0+}
. There is a canonical isomorphism between
(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{F})^{*}
and
\mathfrak{t}^{*}(k)_{0}/\mathfrak{t}^{*}(k)_{0+}=\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
, which gives
\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}
an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-structure.

We denote again by
\mathrm{v}:E\rightarrow\mathrm{v}(E):=\frac{1}{m}\mathbb{Z}\cup\{\infty\}
the extension of the fixed valuation
\mathrm{v}:K\rightarrow\mathrm{v}(K):=\mathbb{Z}\cup\{\infty\}
to
E
. We know that
\mathcal{B}(G,k)=\mathcal{B}(G_{K},K)^{\Gamma}
. Let
x\in\mathcal{A}(T,k)
. There is a
G(K)
-equivariant injection
\mathcal{B}(G_{K},K)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{B}(G_{E},E)
, and we denote the image of
x
under this map to be
x
as well. We can define Moy-Prasad filtrations of
G(E)
,
\mathfrak{g}(E)
and
\mathfrak{g}^{*}(E)
, using the valuation
\mathrm{v}
instead of the normalized one (
m\cdot\mathrm{v}
). We denote by
(\mathbb{G}_{E})_{x}
the reductive quotient at
x
, i.e., the
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-group such that
(\mathbb{G}_{E})_{x}(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q})=G(E)_{x,0}/G(E)_{x,0+}
. Let
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{x}=\mathrm{Lie}((\mathbb{G}_{E})_{x})
and
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{x}^{*}
be it’s dual. Let
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{x,r}=G(E)_{x,r}/G(E)_{x,r+}\cong\mathfrak{g}(E)_{x,r}/\mathfrak{g}(E)_{x,r+}
and
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{E})_{x,-r}=\mathfrak{g}^{*}(E)_{x,-r}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(E)_{x,(-r)+}
. Since
x\in\mathcal{A}(T,k)
,
\mathfrak{F}_{\gamma}(x)=\mathfrak{F}(x)=x
, and
\mathfrak{F}
preserves
G(K)_{x,0}
inducing the
\mathbb{F}_{q}
structure on
\mathbb{G}_{x}
with
\mathbb{G}_{x}^{F}=G(k)_{x,0}/G(k)_{x,0+}
. Similarly,
\mathfrak{F}_{\gamma}
induces an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
- structure on
(\mathbb{G}_{E})_{x}
and
(\mathbb{G}_{E})_{x}^{F}=(\mathbb{G}_{E})_{x}(\mathbb{F}_{q})=G(E^{\prime})_{x,0}/G(E^{\prime})_{x,0+}
.
\mathfrak{F}_{\gamma}
also induces an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-structure on
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{x,r}
and
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{E})_{x,-r}
, with
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{x,r}(\mathbb{F}_{q})=G(E^{\prime})_{x,r}/G(E^{\prime})_{x,r+}\cong\mathfrak{g}(E^{\prime})_{x,r}/\mathfrak{g}(E^{\prime})_{x,r+}
and
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{E})_{x,-r}(\mathbb{F}_{q})=\mathfrak{g}^{*}(E^{\prime})_{x,-r}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(E^{\prime})_{x,(-r)+}
. There is a
(\mathbb{G}_{E})_{x}
-equivariant isomorphism

((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{x,r})^{*}\cong(\mathfrak{g}(E)_{x,r}/\mathfrak{g}(E)_{x,r+})^{*}\cong\mathfrak{g}^{*}(E)_{x,-r}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(E)_{x,(-r)+}

(4.3.1)

defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, and we henceforth use
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{x,r}^{*}
to denote it. Similarly, there is a
(\mathbb{G}_{E})_{x}^{F}
-equivariant isomorphism

((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})^{F}_{x,r})^{*}\cong\left(\mathfrak{g}(E^{\prime})_{x,r}/\mathfrak{g}(E^{\prime})_{x,r+}\right)^{*}\cong\mathfrak{g}^{*}(E^{\prime})_{x,-r}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(E^{\prime})_{x,(-r)+}

(4.3.2)

and hence
((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})^{F}_{x,r})^{*}
,
((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{x,r}^{*})^{F}
and
\mathfrak{g}^{*}(E^{\prime})_{x,-r}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(E^{\prime})_{x,(-r)+}
are canonically isomorphic. So, we will use
((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})^{F}_{x,r})^{*}
to denote them without any ambiguity.

The inclusion
G(K)\xhookrightarrow{\iota}G(E)
maps
G(K)_{x,r}
into
G(E)_{x,r}
, and this induces an injection at the level of
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
points

\iota_{E}(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}):\mathbb{G}_{x}(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q})=G(K)_{x,0}/G(K)_{x,0+}\longrightarrow G(E)_{x,0}/G(E)_{x,0+}=(\mathbb{G}_{E})_{x}(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q})

(4.3.3)

which gives a map of algebraic groups
\iota_{E}:\mathbb{G}_{x}\longrightarrow(\mathbb{G}_{E})_{x}
. As noted in
[
Fin19
]
Section 2.6, this is a closed immersion if
p\neq 2
.

Lemma 4.8
.

For
p\neq 2
and every
r\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, there is an injection

\iota_{E,r}:(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{x,r})^{*}\hookrightarrow(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{x,r}^{*}

(4.3.4)

such that
\iota_{E}(\mathbb{G}_{x})
preserves
\iota_{E,r}((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{x,r})^{*})
and we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{x}\times(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{x,r})^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{x,r})^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{G}_{E})_{x}\times(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{x,r}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{x,r}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{E}\times\iota_{E,r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{E,r}}

(4.3.5)

Moreover, the maps in the diagram are compatible with the respective
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-structures and hence descends to morphisms over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
.

Proof.

\iota_{E,r}
is induced by the inclusion
\mathfrak{g}^{*}(K)\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{*}(K)\otimes_{K}E\cong\mathfrak{g}^{*}(E)
. Note that equivalently we can consider it to be induced by the natural projection map of
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-vector spaces
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{x,r}\rightarrow\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{x,r}
. So,
\iota_{E,r}
is well-defined. Further, if
r\in\mathrm{v}(K)\subset\mathrm{v}(E)
,
\mathfrak{t}^{*}(K)\cap\mathfrak{g}^{*}(K)_{x,-r}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(K)_{x,(-r)+}
maps injectively into
\mathfrak{t}^{*}(E)\cap\mathfrak{g}^{*}(E)_{x,-r}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(E)_{x,(-r)+}
, and for
r\not\in\mathrm{v}(K)
,
\mathfrak{t}^{*}(K)\cap\mathfrak{g}^{*}(K)_{x,-r}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(K)_{x,(-r)+}=\{0\}
. Let
\mathfrak{g}^{*}_{\alpha}
be defined as in
[
MP94
]
. Observing that
\mathfrak{g}^{*}_{\alpha}
can be identified with the dual of
\mathfrak{g}_{-\alpha}
, we see that the map
\mathfrak{g}^{*}_{\alpha}(K)\cap\mathfrak{g}^{*}(K)_{x,-r}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(K)_{x,(-r)+}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{*}_{\alpha}(E)\cap\mathfrak{g}^{*}(E)_{x,-r}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(E)_{x,(-r)+}
is injective for
r-\alpha(x)\in\mathrm{v}(K)\subset\mathrm{v}(E)
, and
\mathfrak{g}^{*}_{\alpha}(K)\cap\mathfrak{g}^{*}(K)_{x,-r}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(K)_{x,(-r)+}=\{0\}
for
r-\alpha(x)\not\in\mathrm{v}(K)
. This gives that the map
\iota_{E,r}
is injective. Once we have this, the diagram commutes essentially because both the top and the bottom horizontal action maps are induced by the co-adjoint action of
G
on
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
.

Now, since
\mathfrak{F}_{\gamma}(x)=\mathfrak{F}(x)=x
, the top and the bottom horizontal maps descend to
\mathbb{F}_{q}
with the given
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-structure by unramified descent. So, if we can show that the Frobenius structure is compatible with
\iota_{E}
and
\iota_{E,r}
, we are done. It is enough to check these properties at the level of
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-points. Let
\mathfrak{F}^{\prime}
and
(\mathfrak{F}_{\gamma})^{\prime}
denote the maps
G(K)\rightarrow G(K)
and
G(E)\rightarrow G(E)
induced by
\mathfrak{F}
and
\mathfrak{F}_{\gamma}
respectively, and
i:K\hookrightarrow E
denote the inclusion. In order to show that the Frobenius
F
commutes with
\iota_{E}(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q})
, it is enough to show that
\iota\circ\mathfrak{F}^{\prime}=(\mathfrak{F}_{\gamma})^{\prime}\circ\iota
. This equivalent to showing
i\circ\mathfrak{F}=\mathfrak{F}_{\gamma}\circ i
, which is true since
\mathfrak{F}_{\gamma}|_{K}=\mathfrak{F}
. Similarly, the case of
\iota_{E,r}
also boils down to the exact same statement using the inclusion
\mathfrak{g}^{*}(K)\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{*}(E)
instead of
\iota
, and we are done.
∎

Consider the map of
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-varieties

(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{x,r})^{*}\hookrightarrow(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{x,r}^{*}\rightarrow(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{x,r}^{*}//(\mathbb{G}_{E})_{x}

(4.3.6)

Using Lemma
4.8
, we see that the map factors through

(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{x,r})^{*}
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{x,r}^{*}
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{x,r}^{*}//(\mathbb{G}_{E})_{x}
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{x,r})^{*}//\mathbb{G}_{x}

(4.3.7)

Further, each of the maps in the above two equations is defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, and hence we also get a map of their
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-rational (or
F
-fixed) points

(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{x,r}^{F})^{*}
((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})^{F}_{x,r})^{*}
\left((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{x,r}^{*}//(\mathbb{G}_{E})_{x}\right)^{F}
\left((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{x,r})^{*}//\mathbb{G}_{x}\right)^{F}

(4.3.8)

Let
r\in\frac{1}{m}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\subset\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
. Note that for
\sigma\in[\mathscr{A}_{m}]
,
G(E)_{x,r}
,
\mathfrak{g}(E)_{x,r}
,
\mathfrak{g}^{*}(E)_{x,r}
does not vary for
x\in\sigma
, and hence
(\mathbb{G}_{E})_{\sigma}
,
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{\sigma}=\mathrm{Lie}((\mathbb{G}_{E})_{\sigma})
,
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{\sigma,r}
,
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})^{*}_{\sigma,r}
and the other similar objects are well-defined. Also, since
\sigma\subset\mathscr{A}
, each of these objects have an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-structure exactly as described before. Further, for any
\frac{i}{m}\in\frac{1}{m}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, there are
(\mathbb{G}_{E})_{\sigma}
-equivariant isomorphisms of Moy-Prasad filtration quotients

\mathfrak{g}(E)_{\sigma,\frac{i}{m}}/\mathfrak{g}(E)_{\sigma,\frac{i}{m}+}\xlongrightarrow{\times\gamma^{-i}}\mathfrak{g}(E)_{\sigma,0}/\mathfrak{g}(E)_{\sigma,0+}

(4.3.9)

and

\mathfrak{g}^{*}(E)_{\sigma,-\frac{i}{m}}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(E)_{\sigma,-\frac{i}{m}+}\xlongrightarrow{\times\gamma^{i}}\mathfrak{g}^{*}(E)_{\sigma,0}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(E)_{\sigma,0+}.

(4.3.10)

The action of
G(E)_{\sigma,0}/G(E)_{\sigma,0+}
on
\mathfrak{g}(E)_{\sigma,\frac{i}{m}}/\mathfrak{g}(E)_{\sigma,\frac{i}{m}+}
(and
\mathfrak{g}^{*}(E)_{\sigma,-\frac{i}{m}}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(E)_{\sigma,-\frac{i}{m}+}
) induced by the adjoint (resp. co-adjoint) action of
G
on
\mathfrak{g}
(resp.
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
) is isomorphic to the adjoint (resp. co-adjoint) action of
(\mathbb{G}_{E})_{\sigma}
on
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{\sigma}
(resp.
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{\sigma}^{*}
). This action is defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, and the action of
G(E^{\prime})_{\sigma,0}/G(E^{\prime})_{\sigma,0+}
on
\mathfrak{g}^{*}(E^{\prime})_{\sigma,-\frac{i}{m}}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(E^{\prime})_{\sigma,-\frac{i}{m}+}
is isomorphic to the
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-points of the co-adjoint action
(\mathbb{G}_{E})_{\sigma}
on
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})^{*}_{\sigma}
.

Since we have fixed
m
and hence
E
, for the next part of this section we define
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}:=(\mathbb{G}_{E})_{\sigma}
and
\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}=\mathfrak{g}(E)_{\sigma,0}/\mathfrak{g}(E)_{\sigma,0+}\cong(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{\sigma}=\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma})
in order to simplify the notation. Further, we also fix
\gamma\in\mathfrak{O}_{E}
and hence
E^{\prime}
. Note that if
m=1
, i.e.,
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, then
E=K
, and
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\cong\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}
and
\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}\cong\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma}
.
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}
is a connected reductive
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-group defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
with root system
\Phi_{\sigma}
,
W_{\sigma}=N_{\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}}(\mathbb{T})/\mathbb{T}
and
\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}
embeds into
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}
as a generalized Levi subgroup. Also, for
\sigma\preceq\tau
,
\mathbb{L}_{\tau}\subset\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}
is an
F
-stable Levi subgroup.

Let
\sigma,\tau,\tau^{\prime}\in[\mathscr{A}_{m}]
be such that
\sigma\preceq\tau
,
\sigma\preceq\tau^{\prime}
and
\tau
,
\tau^{\prime}
are
\sigma
-opposite. We have

\mathbb{U}_{\sigma,\tau}(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q})=G(E)_{\tau,0+}/G(E)_{\sigma,0+}\subset G(E)_{\tau,0}/G(E)_{\sigma,0+}=\mathbb{P}_{\sigma,\tau}(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q})

where
\mathbb{P}_{\sigma,\tau}
is an
F
-stable parabolic subgroup with unipotent radical
R_{u}(\mathbb{P}_{\sigma,\tau})=\mathbb{U}_{\sigma,\tau}
and Levi quotient
\mathbb{L}_{\tau}\cong\mathbb{P}_{\sigma,\tau}/\mathbb{U}_{\sigma,\tau}
, and hence a Levi decomposition
\mathbb{P}_{\sigma,\tau}=\mathbb{U}_{\sigma,\tau}\rtimes\mathbb{L}_{\tau}
.
\mathbb{P}_{\sigma,\tau}
and
\mathbb{U}_{\sigma,\tau}
have
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-structures given by
\mathbb{U}_{\sigma,\tau}(\mathbb{F}_{q})=G(E^{\prime})_{\tau,0+}/G(E^{\prime})_{\sigma,0+}
and
\mathbb{P}_{\sigma,\tau}(\mathbb{F}_{q})=G(E^{\prime})_{\tau,0}/G(E^{\prime})_{\sigma,0+}
. Note that for
\sigma\preceq\tau
,
\mathbb{L}_{\tau}
is basically the unique Levi subgroup in
\mathbb{P}_{\sigma,\tau}
containing
\mathbb{T}
, and
\mathbb{P}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime}}
is the opposite parabolic subgroup in
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}
with unipotent radical
\mathbb{U}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime}}
. Since
\tau,\tau^{\prime}
are
\sigma
-opposite, we have
\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\tau}\cong\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\tau^{\prime}}
. Let
\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau}=\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbb{U}_{\sigma,\tau})
and
\bar{\mathfrak{p}}_{\sigma,\tau}=\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbb{P}_{\sigma,\tau})=\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\tau}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau}
. We have
\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}=\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\tau}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime}}=\bar{\mathfrak{p}}_{\sigma,\tau}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime}}
.

For
r\in\frac{1}{m}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, we see from (
4.3.9
) that there is an isomorphism
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{\sigma,r}\xlongrightarrow{\eta^{\sigma}_{\gamma}}\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}
. From the statement and the proof of Proposition
4.2
and remarks following it, we note that there is a decomposition

(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{\sigma,r}=(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{E})_{\sigma,\tau,r}\oplus(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{\tau,r}\oplus(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{E})_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}

where
(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{E})_{\sigma,\tau,r}=G(E)_{\tau,r+}/G(E)_{\sigma,r+}\cong\mathfrak{g}(E)_{\tau,r+}/\mathfrak{g}(E)_{\sigma,r+}
. The isomorphism
\eta^{\sigma}_{\gamma}
sends
(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{E})_{\sigma,\tau,r}\rightarrow\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau}
and
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{\tau,r}\rightarrow\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\tau}
and is compatible with the given
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-structures. So,
\eta^{\sigma}_{\gamma}|_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{\tau,r}}=\eta^{\tau}_{\gamma}
and from (
4.3.10
) we also have a similar isomorphism
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})^{*}_{\sigma,r}\xlongrightarrow{\theta^{\sigma}_{\gamma}}\bar{\mathfrak{l}}^{*}_{\sigma}
.

Remark 4.9
.

The
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-structures and the isomorphisms depend upon the choice of the uniformizer
\gamma\in\mathfrak{O}_{E}
, and hence we have used a subscript of
\gamma
in the notation of the same.

We have
(\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{F})^{*}=(\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{*})^{F}
, and using the co-adjoint action of
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}
on
\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{*}
and Theorem 4 in
[
KW76
]
( which works for general connected reductive groups instead of just almost simple ones, check
[
CDT25
, Section,4.1]
), we have a map of
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-varieties

\bar{\chi}_{\sigma}:\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{*}\longrightarrow\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{*}//\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\cong\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma}

and hence a map between their
F
-fixed (or
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-rational points)

\chi_{\sigma}:(\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{F})^{*}\cong(\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{*})^{F}\longrightarrow(\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{*}//\mathbb{L}_{\sigma})^{F}\xlongrightarrow{\simeq}(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma})^{F}.

(4.3.11)

Remark 4.10
.

The isomorphism
\mathcal{O}(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*})^{W_{\sigma}}\cong\mathcal{O}(\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{*})^{\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}}
is induced by the inclusion
\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}\hookrightarrow\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{*}
which is defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, and hence
\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma}\xrightarrow{\sim}\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{*}//\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}
is defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
(cf for example
[
Mil17
]
, Corollary 4.34). Since
\bar{\mathfrak{l}}^{*}_{\sigma}
and
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}
are defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
,
\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{*}\longrightarrow\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{*}//\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}
induced by
\mathcal{O}(\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{*})^{\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}(\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{*})
is also defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
. So, the map
\bar{\chi}_{\sigma}
is defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
and we have a map of
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-rational points
\chi_{\sigma}
.

From Lemma
4.8
, we see that there is a map of
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
vector spaces
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r})^{*}\hookrightarrow(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{\sigma,r}^{*}\xlongrightarrow{\theta_{\gamma}^{\sigma}}\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}
which is compatible with the
\mathbb{F}_{q}
structures, and hence a map of
F
-fixed points

p^{*}_{\sigma,r}:(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}^{F}_{\sigma,r})^{*}\hookrightarrow((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})^{F}_{\sigma,r})^{*}\longrightarrow(\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{F})^{*}

The map
p^{*}_{\sigma,r}
can equivalently be described as being induced by the projection map of
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-vector spaces
\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{F}\xrightarrow{\simeq}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})^{F}_{\sigma,r}\rightarrow\bar{\mathfrak{g}}^{F}_{\sigma,r}
. Let
\chi_{\sigma,r}:=\chi_{\sigma}\circ p_{\sigma,r}^{*}
. Since the isomorphism
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})^{*}_{\sigma,r}\xlongrightarrow{\theta^{\sigma}_{\gamma}}\bar{\mathfrak{l}}^{*}_{\sigma}
is
(\mathbb{G}_{E})_{\sigma}=\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}
-equivariant and defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, we have
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{\sigma,r}^{*}//\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\cong\bar{\mathfrak{l}}^{*}_{\sigma}//\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}
defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
and using (
4.3.7
), the following diagram commutes.

(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r})^{*}
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{\sigma,r}^{*}
\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{*}
\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{*}//\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r})^{*}//\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}
\simeq

Since all the above maps are defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, we see using (
4.3.8
) that the map
\chi_{\sigma,r}:(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}^{F}_{\sigma,r})^{*}\longrightarrow(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma})^{F}
factors as

(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}^{F}_{\sigma,r})^{*}
((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})^{F}_{\sigma,r})^{*}
(\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{F})^{*}
(\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{*}//\mathbb{L}_{\sigma})^{F}
((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r})^{*}//\mathbb{G}_{\sigma})^{F}
(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma})^{F}
\simeq
\simeq

(4.3.12)

Let
\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma})^{F}]
be the space of complex valued functions on
(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma})^{F}

with multiplication given by pointwise multiplication of functions. The function
\mathbbm{1}
which takes a value of
1
at every point is the identity element. For any
\sigma\preceq\tau
,
W_{\tau}\subset W_{\sigma}
and we have a canonical map
\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\tau}\longrightarrow\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma}
compatible with
the Frobenious endomorphism. We denote by

\mathrm{res}^{\sigma}_{\tau}:\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma})^{F}]\to\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\tau})^{F}]

the map given by pull back along the natural
map
t_{\sigma,\tau}:(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\tau})^{F}\longrightarrow(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma})^{F}
.

Definition 4.11
.

We define
f\in C(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})
to be stable if
\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f):(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
factors through

(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}\xlongrightarrow{\chi_{\sigma,r}}(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma})^{F}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}.

We use
C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})
to denote stable functions on
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}
. Using properties of Fourier transforms, we immediately see that
C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})\subset C(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})
forms a subalgebra with unit
|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}|^{1/2}\mathbbm{1}_{0}
.

The following lemma is essential in proving certain properties of stable functions, which we will state after this.

Lemma 4.12
.

Let
\mathrm{G}=\mathrm{L}\mathrm{U}
be a Levi decomposition of a parabolic subgroup
\mathrm{P}
of a connected reductive algebraic group
\mathrm{G}
. Let
\mathfrak{P}=\mathfrak{L}\oplus\mathfrak{U}
be the corresponding decomposition of the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{P}=\mathrm{Lie}(\mathrm{P})
. For,
X\in\mathfrak{L}^{*}
and
N\in\mathfrak{U}^{*}
, we have
(X+N)_{s}=\mathrm{Ad}^{*}(v)(X_{s})
for some
v\in\mathrm{U}
and
X_{s}
defined as in
[
KW76
]
Section 3, where
\mathrm{Ad}^{*}
denotes the co-adjoint action.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{G}=\mathrm{Lie}(\mathrm{G})
. We are working with the Jordan decomposition for the dual of the Lie algebra of a connected algebraic group as defined in Section 3 of
[
KW76
]
. There exists a Borel
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{L}}=\mathrm{T}\mathrm{V}
of
\mathrm{L}
such that
X_{s}\in\mathrm{Lie}(\mathrm{T})^{*}=\mathfrak{T}^{*}
and
X_{n}\in\mathrm{Lie}(\mathrm{V})^{*}
. We consider
\mathfrak{L}^{*}
,
\mathfrak{P}^{*}
,
\mathfrak{T}^{*}
and other duals embedded into
\mathfrak{G}^{*}
as in
[
KW76
]
. Then,
\mathrm{B}=\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{L}}\mathrm{U}=\mathrm{T}\mathrm{V}\mathrm{U}
is a Borel subgroup of
\mathrm{G}
, and the Lie algebra
\mathrm{Lie}(\mathrm{B})=\mathrm{Lie}(\mathrm{T})\oplus\mathrm{Lie}(\mathrm{V})\oplus\mathrm{Lie}(\mathrm{U})
and hence
\mathrm{Lie}(\mathrm{B})^{*}=\mathfrak{T}^{*}\oplus\mathrm{Lie}(\mathrm{V})^{*}\oplus\mathfrak{U}^{*}
.

Now,
X+N\in\mathrm{Lie}(\mathrm{B})^{*}
and using Jordan decomposition for duals, we see that
(X+N)_{s}=\mathrm{Ad}^{*}(v)(Y)
for
Y\in\mathfrak{T}^{*}
and
v\in\mathrm{B}
. Also, without loss of generality, we can assume that
b\in\mathrm{V}\mathrm{U}
since
\mathrm{T}
acts via identity on
\mathfrak{T}^{*}
. Consider the map

\varphi_{v}:=\mathrm{Ad}^{*}(v)-Id:\mathrm{Lie}(\mathrm{B})^{*}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Lie}(\mathrm{B})^{*}

\mathfrak{T}^{*}
embedds into
\mathrm{Lie}(\mathrm{B})^{*}
as
\mathfrak{T}^{*}=\{a\in\mathrm{Lie}(\mathrm{B})^{*}\>|\>a(x)=0\>\forall\>x\in\mathrm{Lie}(\mathrm{V})\oplus\mathfrak{U}\}
. We claim that
\varphi_{v}(\mathfrak{T}^{*})\subset\mathrm{Lie}(\mathrm{V})^{*}\oplus\mathfrak{U}^{*}
. Let
a\in\mathfrak{T}^{*}
and
x\in\mathfrak{T}
.
\varphi_{v}(a)(x)=a((\mathrm{Ad}(v^{-1})-Id)(x))
. Using Proposition 3.7 of
[
Let96
]
, we see that
(\mathrm{Ad}(v^{-1})-Id)|_{\mathfrak{T}}
for
v\in\mathrm{V}\mathrm{U}
is a derivative of the map
\mu_{v^{-1}}:\mathrm{T}\rightarrow\mathrm{V}\mathrm{U}
defined by
\mu_{v^{-1}}(t)=t^{-1}v^{-1}tv
, and hence a map
(\mathrm{Ad}(v^{-1})-Id):\mathfrak{T}\rightarrow\mathrm{Lie}(\mathrm{V})\oplus\mathfrak{U}
. Thus,
\varphi_{v}(a)(x)=0\>\forall\>x\in\mathfrak{T}\Rightarrow\varphi_{v}(a)\in\mathrm{Lie}(\mathrm{V})^{*}\oplus\mathfrak{U}^{*}
, which proves our claim.

Using the above claim, we see that
\mathrm{Ad}^{*}(v)(Y)-Y\in\mathrm{Lie}(\mathrm{V})^{*}\oplus\mathfrak{U}^{*}
, and
(X+N)=(X+N)_{s}+(X+N)_{n}\in Y+\mathrm{Lie}(\mathrm{V})^{*}\oplus\mathfrak{U}^{*}
since
\mathrm{V}\mathrm{U}
is normal in
\mathrm{B}
. Further, we also have
X+N=X_{s}+X_{n}+N\in X_{s}+\mathrm{Lie}(\mathrm{V})^{*}\oplus\mathfrak{U}^{*}
. So,
X_{s}=Y
by uniqueness of Jordan decomposition. In the above argument,
\mathrm{L}
could have been replaced by
Z_{\mathrm{L}}(X_{s})
, which shows that
v
can be taken in
\mathrm{U}
and finishes our proof.
∎

Proposition 4.13

(Properties of stable functions)
.

(1)

For any
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma})^{F}]
, the function
f_{\varepsilon}:=\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma.r}^{F})^{*}}\left(\chi_{\sigma,r}^{*}(\varepsilon)^{-}\right)

is a stable function on
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}
, and the assignment
\varepsilon\mapsto f_{\varepsilon}
gives a surjective algebra morphism

\rho_{\sigma,r}:\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma})^{F}]\longrightarrow C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})

which is an isomorphism when
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.

(2)

For
\sigma\preceq\tau\in[\mathscr{A}_{m}]
and
f\in C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})
, we have
\mathrm{Res}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}(f)\in C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})
, and the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma})^{F}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\tau})^{F}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\sigma,r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(c_{\sigma,\tau,r})^{-1}\mathrm{res}^{\sigma}_{\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\tau,r}}

(4.3.13)

where
c_{\sigma,\tau,r}
is as defined in Remark
4.6
.

(3)

For any
f\in C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})
and
\sigma\preceq\tau\in[\mathscr{A}_{m}]
, we have

\sum_{N\in\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}}f(X+N)=0\text{ for }X\not\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}

(4.3.14)

Proof.

(1)

Let
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma})^{F}]
. From equation (
4.3.12
), we see that
\chi_{\sigma,r}^{*}(\varepsilon)^{-}\in C((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*})
and hence
f_{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}(\chi_{\sigma,r}^{*}(\varepsilon)^{-})\in C(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})
. From properties of Fourier transforms in Proposition
4.4

(ii)
, we have that
\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}(\chi_{\sigma,r}^{*}(\varepsilon)^{-})\in C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})
. Proposition
4.4

(iv)
shows that the map
\rho_{\sigma,r}
is an algebra morphism, with a simple calculation showing
\rho_{\sigma,r}(\mathbbm{1})=|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}|^{1/2}\mathbbm{1}_{0}
. Let
f\in C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})
. Then, by the definition of stable functions,
\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f)=\chi_{\sigma,r}^{*}(\varepsilon)\in C((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*})
for some
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma})^{F}]
. Using
4.4

(ii)
, we see that

\rho_{\sigma,r}(\varepsilon)=f_{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}(\chi_{\sigma,r}^{*}(\varepsilon)^{-})=\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f^{-})=f

giving surjectivity of
\rho_{\sigma,r}
.

Observe that for integral depths,
m=1
, and hence we have
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\cong\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}
and
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{\sigma,r}=(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})^{*}_{\sigma,r}
. In order to show that
\rho_{\sigma,r}
is an isomorphism in this case, it is enough to show that
\chi_{\sigma,r}:(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}\rightarrow(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma})^{F}
is surjective. With the identifications made above, this is equivalent to showing that the map of
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-varieties
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{*}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma}^{*}\rightarrow\bar{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{\sigma}//\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}
is surjective at the level of
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-points. Note that each map is defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
and hence we have a map at the level
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma}^{*}(\mathbb{F}_{q})\rightarrow\bar{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{\sigma}//\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
of
\mathbb{F}_{q}
points. Let
\phi
denote the map
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma}^{*}(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q})\rightarrow\bar{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{\sigma}//\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q})
, with the
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
varieties identified with the set of
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-points and let
y\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{\sigma}//\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{F}_{q})\subset\bar{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{\sigma}//\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q})
. The map
\phi
is surjective, and
\phi^{-1}(y)\subset\bar{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{\sigma}
is a union of
\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q})
-orbits. There is a unique closed orbit
O\subset\phi^{-1}(y)
, and by
[
KW76
, Theorem 4]
, it corresponds to a semisimple element. Thus, we have a closed
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-subvariety
O\subset\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma}^{*}
, and
\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q})
acts transitively on
O(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q})
. By Lang’s theorem, we know that if
O
is
F
-stable (i.e., defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
), then
O
has an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-point. Since
y\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{\sigma}//\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
,
\phi^{-1}(y)
is
F
-stable and hence
O
is
F
-stable. Thus,
O(\mathbb{F}_{q})\neq\emptyset
which shows surjectivity of the map
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma}^{*}(\mathbb{F}_{q})\rightarrow\bar{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{\sigma}//\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
, thereby proving our claim.

(2)

Let
\sigma,\tau,\tau^{\prime}\in[\mathscr{A}_{m}]
such that
\sigma\preceq\tau
,
\sigma\preceq\tau^{\prime}
and
\tau,\>\tau^{\prime}
are
\sigma
-opposite. We know that
\bar{\mathfrak{l}}^{*}_{\sigma}\cong\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{*}_{\sigma,\tau}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\tau}^{*}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{*}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime}}
Consider the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\tau}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\tau}^{*}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{*}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\mathfrak{l}}^{*}_{\sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\tau}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\chi}_{\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{pr}_{\sigma,\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{i}_{\sigma,\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\chi}_{\sigma}}

where
{\bar{pr}_{\sigma,\tau}}
is the natural projection and
\bar{i}_{\sigma,\tau}
is the inclusion. By Lemma
4.12
, the diagram commutes. Now,
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{*}\cong\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{*}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{*}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{*}
, where we use projection maps onto subspaces to give an identification of
\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{*}
,
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{*}
and
\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{*}
as subspaces of
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{*}
. We also observe that the map
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{\sigma,r}\xrightarrow{\iota_{E,r}}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})^{*}_{\sigma,r}\xrightarrow{\theta^{\sigma}_{\gamma}}\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{*}
maps
\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{*}\rightarrow\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau}^{*}
,
\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{*}\rightarrow\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime}}^{*}
and
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{*}\rightarrow\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\tau}^{*}
using the fact that
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}\hookrightarrow(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{\sigma,r}
maps
\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}\rightarrow(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{E})_{\sigma,\tau,r}
,
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}\rightarrow(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{\tau,r}
and similar identification of duals. Thus we have the following commutative diagram,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{*}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{*}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{\sigma,r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\tau}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\tau}^{*}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{*}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\mathfrak{l}}^{*}_{\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{pr}_{\sigma,\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{i}_{\sigma,\tau}}

with natural projection and inclusion maps. Combining the above two diagrams, we have

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{*}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{*}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{\sigma,r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\tau}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\mathfrak{l}}^{*}_{\sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\tau}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\chi}_{\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\chi}_{\sigma}}

Further, each of the arrows is compatible with Frobenius endomoprhism, and hence we have a similar commutative diagram for
F
-fixed points.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})^{*}\oplus(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}^{F}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r})^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}^{F}_{\sigma,r})^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\tau}^{F})^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\bar{\mathfrak{l}}^{F}_{\sigma})^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\tau})^{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma})^{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi_{\tau,r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr_{\sigma,\tau,r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\sigma,\tau,r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi_{\sigma,r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi_{\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi_{\sigma}}

(4.3.15)

For
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma})^{F}]
and
X^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})^{*}
, it follows using (
4.3.15
) that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Res}^{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r})^{*}}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r})^{*}}(\chi_{\sigma,r}^{*}(\varepsilon)^{-})(X^{*})

\displaystyle=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F}\right|^{-1}\sum_{N^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F})^{*}}\chi_{\sigma,r}^{*}(\varepsilon)(-X^{*}-N^{*})

\displaystyle=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F}\right|^{-1}\sum_{N^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F})^{*}}\chi_{\sigma,r}^{*}(\varepsilon)(i_{\sigma,\tau,r}(-X^{*}-N^{*}))

\displaystyle=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F}\right|^{-1}\sum_{N^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F})^{*}}(\chi_{\tau,r}\circ pr_{\sigma,\tau,r})^{*}(\mathrm{res}^{\sigma}_{\tau}(\varepsilon))(-X^{*}-N^{*})

\displaystyle=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F}\right|^{-1}\sum_{N^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F})^{*}}\chi_{\tau,r}^{*}(\mathrm{res}^{\sigma}_{\tau}(\varepsilon))(-X^{*})

\displaystyle=\chi_{\tau,r}^{*}(\mathrm{res}^{\sigma}_{\tau}(\varepsilon))(-X^{*})=\chi_{\tau,r}^{*}(\mathrm{res}^{\sigma}_{\tau}(\varepsilon))^{-}(X^{*})

Then, using Proposition
4.5
and Remark
4.6
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Res}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}\circ\rho_{\sigma,r}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Res}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}(\chi_{\sigma,r}^{*}(\varepsilon)^{-})=(c_{\sigma,\tau,r})^{-1}\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})^{*}}\circ\mathrm{Res}^{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r})^{*}}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r})^{*}}(\chi_{\sigma,r}^{*}(\varepsilon)^{-})

\displaystyle=(c_{\sigma,\tau,r})^{-1}\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})^{*}}(\chi_{\tau,r}^{*}(\mathrm{res}^{\sigma}_{\tau}(\varepsilon))^{-})=\rho_{\tau,r}((c_{\sigma,\tau,r})^{-1}\mathrm{res}^{\sigma}_{\tau}(\varepsilon))

which finishes the proof of
(2)
.

(3)

Consider the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}/\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}\cong\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}

with natural inclusions and projections. Given
f\in C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})
, we define
h:\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}/\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}\cong\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
as
h(X)=\sum_{Y\in P^{-1}(X)}f(Y)
. For
X\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F}
,
h(X)=\sum_{N\in\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}}f(X+N)
. If we can show that
h
is supported on
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}
, we are done. Let
V=\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F}
and consider the Fourier transform of
h
defined as in (
4.2.3
). Then, for
X^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})^{*}\oplus(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F})^{*}

\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f)(X^{*})=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right|^{-1/2}\sum_{Y\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F}}\tilde{\psi}(X^{*}(Y))h(Y)=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}\right|^{1/2}\mathcal{F}_{V}(h)(X^{*})

For
f\in C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})
, we have from (
4.3.15
) that
\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(f)|_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})^{*}\oplus(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F})^{*}}
factors through

(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})^{*}\oplus(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F})^{*}\xlongrightarrow{p_{\sigma,\tau,r}}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})^{*}\xlongrightarrow{\chi_{\tau,r}}(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\tau})^{F}\longrightarrow(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma})^{F}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}

and thus is constant on the fibres of the projection
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})^{*}\oplus(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F})^{*}\xlongrightarrow{p_{\sigma,\tau,r}}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})^{*}
, and
\tilde{h}=\mathcal{F}_{V}(h)
has the same property. In order to show that
h
is supported on
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}
, it is enough to show the same for
\mathcal{F}_{V^{*}}(\tilde{h})=h^{-}
. For
X_{1}+X_{2}\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F}

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{V^{*}}(\tilde{h})(X_{1}+X_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}Y_{1}^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})^{*}\\
Y_{2}^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F})^{*}\end{subarray}}}\tilde{\psi}(Y_{1}^{*}(X_{1})+Y_{2}^{*}(X_{2}))\tilde{h}(Y_{1}^{*}+Y_{2}^{*})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}Y_{1}^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})^{*}\\
Y_{2}^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F})^{*}\end{subarray}}}\tilde{\psi}(Y_{1}^{*}(X_{1}))\tilde{\psi}(Y_{2}^{*}(X_{2}))\tilde{h}(Y_{1}^{*})

\displaystyle=\sum_{Y_{1}^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})^{*}}\tilde{\psi}(Y_{1}^{*}(X_{1}))\tilde{h}(Y_{1}^{*})\sum_{Y_{2}^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F})^{*}}\tilde{\psi}(Y_{2}^{*}(X_{2}))

Thus,
\mathcal{F}_{V^{*}}(\tilde{h})=h^{-}=0
if
X_{2}\neq 0
, and proves that
h
is supported on
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}
.

∎

Proposition 4.14
.

Let
\sigma\in[\mathscr{A}_{m}]
and
n\in N_{G}(T)(k)
. Then the adjoint action of
n
induces an isomorphism

C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})\xlongrightarrow{\simeq}C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{n\sigma,r}^{F})

Proof.

The co-adjoint action of
n
induces an isomorphism
\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}\xlongrightarrow{\mathrm{Ad}^{*}(n)}\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}
equivariant with respect to
W_{\sigma}
-action (where
W_{\sigma}
acts on the r.h.s via the isomorphism
W_{\sigma}\xlongrightarrow{\mathrm{Ad}(n)}W_{n\sigma}
), and thus we have an isomorphism
\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma}\xlongrightarrow[\mathrm{Ad}^{*}(n)]{\simeq}\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{n\sigma}
. Since
nG_{\sigma,r}n^{-1}=G_{n\sigma}
for all
r\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
, we immediately see that the following diagram commutes.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma})^{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{n\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{n\sigma})^{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi_{\sigma,r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ad}^{*}(n)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ad}^{*}(n)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi_{n\sigma,r}}

Let
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma})^{F}]
and
\prescript{n}{}{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{n\sigma})^{F}]
such that
\prescript{n}{}{\varepsilon}(x)=\varepsilon(\mathrm{Ad}^{*}(n^{-1})x)
. So,
\prescript{n}{}{\varepsilon}=(\mathrm{Ad}^{*}(n^{-1}))^{*}(\varepsilon)
. Let us similarly use
\prescript{n^{-1}}{}{}X
to denote
\mathrm{Ad}(n^{-1})X\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}
for
X\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{n\sigma,r}^{F}
etc. Then,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{n\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}(\chi_{n\sigma,r}^{*}(\prescript{n}{}{\varepsilon})^{-})(X)

\displaystyle=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{n\sigma,r}^{F}\right|^{-1/2}\sum_{Y^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{n\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\tilde{\psi}(Y^{*}(X))\prescript{n}{}{\varepsilon}(\chi_{n\sigma,r}(-Y^{*}))

\displaystyle=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right|^{-1/2}\sum_{Y^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{n\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\tilde{\psi}(Y^{*}(X))\varepsilon(\chi_{\sigma,r}(-\mathrm{Ad}^{*}(n^{-1})Y^{*}))

\displaystyle=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right|^{-1/2}\sum_{Y^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{n\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\tilde{\psi}(\prescript{n^{-1}}{}{}Y^{*}(\prescript{n^{-1}}{}{X}))\varepsilon(\chi_{\sigma,r}(-\prescript{n^{-1}}{}{Y}^{*}))

\displaystyle=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right|^{-1/2}\sum_{Y_{1}^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\tilde{\psi}(Y_{1}^{*}(\prescript{n^{-1}}{}{X}))\varepsilon(\chi_{\sigma,r}(-Y_{1}^{*}))

\displaystyle=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right|^{-1/2}\sum_{Y_{1}^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\tilde{\psi}(Y_{1}^{*}(\prescript{n^{-1}}{}{X}))\chi_{\sigma,r}^{*}(\varepsilon)^{-}(Y_{1}^{*}))

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}_{(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}(\chi_{\sigma,r}^{*}(\varepsilon)^{-})(\prescript{n^{-1}}{}{X})=\mathrm{Ad}(n^{-1})^{*}(\rho_{\sigma,r}(\varepsilon))(X)

which shows that the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma})^{F}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{n\sigma})^{F}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{n\sigma,r}^{F})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{Ad}^{*}(n^{-1}))^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\sigma,r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ad}(n^{-1})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{n\sigma,r}}

(4.3.16)

commutes and finishes the proof.
∎

5
From Stable functions to positive depth Bernstein center

We will use the theory of stable functions developed in the previous section to construct elements in the depth-
r
center for
r\in\frac{1}{m}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\subset\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
, and attach parameters to smooth irreducible representations, which only depends upon the Moy-Prasad type of the representation.

Let
W=N_{G}(T)/T
be the Weyl group of
G
, and
\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]
be the algebra of complex-valued functions on
(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}
. We again fix
\varpi\in\mathfrak{O}_{k}
,
E
and
\gamma\in\mathfrak{O}_{E}
with
\gamma^{m}=\varpi
as in Section
4.3
. We will construct a map from
\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]
to the depth-
r
Bernstein center
\mathcal{Z}^{r}(G)
, using our description of
\mathcal{Z}^{r}(G)
in Section
3
. Let
\sigma\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{m}]
. Note that
W_{\sigma}=N_{\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}}(\mathbb{T})/\mathbb{T}
agrees with the image of
N_{G(E)_{\sigma,0}}(T)
in
W
(see
[
Deb24
]
, Lemma 7.2.1), and hence there is an embedding
W_{\sigma}\hookrightarrow W
. Thus, we have a map

t_{\sigma}:(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma})^{F}\longrightarrow(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}

We have a natural inclusion map
