\iota_{\sigma,r}:C(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})\rightarrow C_{c}^{\infty}\left(\frac{G_{\sigma,r}/G_{\sigma,r+}}{G_{\sigma,0}}\right)\rightarrow C_{c}^{\infty}\left(\frac{G(k)/G_{\sigma,r+}}{G_{\sigma,0}}\right)=\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\sigma}

sending
C(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})\ni f:\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
to
\iota_{\sigma,r}(f)\in\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\sigma}
supported in
G_{\sigma,r}\subset G(k)
given by

\iota_{\sigma,r}(f):G_{\sigma,r}\rightarrow G_{\sigma,r}/G_{\sigma,r+}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\xrightarrow{f}\mathbb{C}

We define
c^{\mu}_{\sigma,r}
to be the constant
\mu(G_{\sigma,r+})\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right|^{1/2}
. Consider the composed map

j_{\sigma,r}:\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]\xlongrightarrow{(c^{\mu}_{\sigma,r})^{-1}t^{*}_{\sigma}}\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma})^{F}]\xlongrightarrow{\rho_{\sigma,r}}C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})\xlongrightarrow{\iota_{\sigma,r}}\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\sigma}

Observe that the map
j_{\sigma,r}
is an algebra homomorphism sending the unit
\mathbbm{1}\in\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]
to
\delta_{G_{\sigma,r+}}\in\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\sigma}
.

Remark 5.1
.

Note that some of the the individual maps in the definition of
j_{\sigma,r}
are just vector space maps, although the composition is an algebra morphism.

Our main idea is to show that the following diagrams commute and use that to construct a map into
\mathcal{Z}^{r}(G)
, using the limit description. Let
\sigma,\tau\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{m}]
,
\sigma\preceq\tau
. Then, we have

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\sigma,r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\tau,r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{r}_{\sigma,\tau}}

Further, let
n\in N_{G}(T)(k)
such that
n\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}
and
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{m}]
such that
n\sigma_{1}\preceq\sigma_{2}
. Then

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\sigma_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\sigma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\sigma_{1},r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\sigma_{2},r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{r}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},n}}

We do that in several steps using a series of lemmas.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
\sigma,\tau\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{m}]
such that
\sigma\preceq\tau
. Then
c^{\mu}_{\sigma,r}\cdot c_{\sigma,\tau,r}=c^{\mu}_{\tau,r}

Proof.

\frac{c^{\mu}_{\tau,r}}{c^{\mu}_{\sigma,r}}=\frac{\mu(G_{\tau,r+})}{\mu(G_{\sigma,r+})}\cdot\frac{\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}\right|^{1/2}}{\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right|^{1/2}}=\frac{\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}\right|\cdot\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}\right|^{1/2}}{\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}\right|^{1/2}\cdot\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}\right|^{1/2}\cdot\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau^{\prime},r}^{F}\right|^{1/2}}=c_{\sigma,\tau,r}

∎

Lemma 5.3
.

For
\sigma\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{m}]
, we have a map

i_{\sigma,r}:\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]\longrightarrow C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})

such that for
\sigma\preceq\tau\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{m}]
, we have the following commutative diagram.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\sigma,r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\tau,r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}}

(5.0.1)

Proof.

For
\sigma\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{m}]
, define
i_{\sigma,r}:\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]\longrightarrow C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})
as the composition

\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]\xlongrightarrow{(c^{\mu}_{\sigma,r})^{-1}t^{*}_{\sigma}}\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma})^{F}]\xlongrightarrow{\rho_{\sigma,r}}C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})

For
\sigma\preceq\tau\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{m}]
, it immediately follows from the previous lemma that the following diagram commutes.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma})^{F}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\tau})^{F}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(c^{\mu}_{\sigma,r})^{-1}t^{*}_{\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(c^{\mu}_{\tau,r})^{-1}t^{*}_{\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(c_{\sigma,\tau,r})^{-1}\mathrm{res}^{\sigma}_{\tau}}

Combining the above diagram and (
4.3.13
), we get

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma})^{F}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\tau})^{F}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(c^{\mu}_{\sigma,r})^{-1}t^{*}_{\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(c^{\mu}_{\tau,r})^{-1}t^{*}_{\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(c_{\sigma,\tau,r})^{-1}\mathrm{res}^{\sigma}_{\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\sigma,r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\tau,r}}

which proves the lemma since
i_{\sigma,r}=\rho_{\sigma,r}\circ(c^{\mu}_{\sigma,r})^{-1}t^{*}_{\sigma}
.
∎

Lemma 5.4
.

For
\sigma\preceq\tau\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{m}]
, the map
\iota_{\sigma,r}:C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\sigma}
fits into the following commutative diagram.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\sigma,r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{r}_{\sigma,\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\tau,r}}

(5.0.2)

Proof.

For
f\in C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})
, we immediately see that
\phi^{r}_{\sigma,\tau}\circ\iota_{\sigma,r}(f)=\iota_{\sigma,r}(f)*\delta_{G_{\tau,r+}}
is supported inside
G_{\sigma,r+}
. Let
x\in G_{\sigma,r+}
with image
\bar{x}\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}
. Then,

\displaystyle\iota_{\sigma,r}(f)*\delta_{G_{\tau,r+}}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mu(G_{\tau,r+})}\int_{G(k)}\iota_{\sigma,r}(f)(xy^{-1})\delta_{G_{\tau,r+}}(y)d\mu(y)=\frac{1}{\mu(G_{\tau,r+})}\int_{G_{\tau,r+}}\iota_{\sigma,r}(f)(xy^{-1})d\mu(y)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu(\delta_{G_{\sigma,r+}})}{\mu(G_{\tau,r+})}\sum_{\bar{u}\in G_{\tau,r+}/G_{\sigma,r+}}\int_{G_{\sigma,r+}}\iota_{\sigma,r}(f)(xuz)d\mu(z)=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}\right|^{-1}\sum_{N\in\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}}f(\bar{x}+N)

Since
f\in C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})
, we know from
4.3.14
that

\left|\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}\right|^{-1}\sum_{N\in\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}}f(\bar{x}+N)=\begin{cases}0,&\text{if }\bar{x}\not\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}\\
\mathrm{Res}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}(f)(\bar{x}),&\text{if }\bar{x}\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}\oplus\bar{\mathfrak{u}}_{\sigma,\tau,r}^{F}\end{cases}

Thus, we see that
\iota_{\sigma,r}(f)*\delta_{G_{\tau,r+}}(x)
is supported on
G_{\tau,r}\subset G_{\sigma,r}
, and for
x\in G_{\tau,r}
with image
\bar{x}\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}^{F}

\phi^{r}_{\sigma,\tau}\circ\iota_{\sigma,r}(f)(x)=\mathrm{Res}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}(f)(\bar{x})=\iota_{\tau,r}\circ\mathrm{Res}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\tau,r}}(f)(x)

which proves the commutativity of the diagram.
∎

Remark 5.5
.

Note that it was essential to consider
C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})
instead of
C(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})
, since the vanishing property (
4.3.14
) was instrumental to the proof.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{m}]
and
n\in N_{G}(T)(k)
such that
n\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}
and
n\sigma_{1}\preceq\sigma_{2}
. Then, we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\sigma_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\sigma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\sigma_{1},r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\sigma_{2},r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{r}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},n}}

Proof.

Since
c^{\mu}_{\sigma_{1},r}=c^{\mu}_{n\sigma_{1},r}
, using (
4.3.16
) , we have

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma_{1}})^{F}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma_{1},r}^{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{n\sigma_{1}})^{F}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{n\sigma_{1},r}^{F})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(c^{\mu}_{\sigma_{1},r})^{-1}t^{*}_{\sigma_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(c^{\mu}_{n\sigma_{1},r})^{-1}t^{*}_{n\sigma_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\sigma_{1},r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{n\sigma_{1},r}}

Then, applying Lemma
5.3
to
n\sigma_{1}\preceq\sigma_{2}
and combining with the above diagram, we have the following.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma_{1},r}^{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{n\sigma_{1},r}^{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma_{2},r}^{F})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\sigma_{1},r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{n\sigma_{1},r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\sigma_{2},r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{n\sigma_{1},r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma_{2},r}}}

Further, the adjoint action of
n
gives us an isomorphism
\mathrm{Ad}(n^{-1})^{*}:\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\sigma_{1}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{M}^{r}_{n\sigma_{1}}
and hence a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma_{1},r}^{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\sigma_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{n\sigma_{1},r}^{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{r}_{n\sigma_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma_{2},r}^{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\sigma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\sigma_{1},r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{n\sigma_{1},r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}^{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{n\sigma_{1},r}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma_{2},r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{r}_{n\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\sigma_{2},r}}

Since
j_{\sigma,r}=\iota_{\sigma,r}\circ i_{\sigma,r}
, combining the above two diagrams proves the lemma.
∎

If
(\pi,V)
is a smooth representation of
G(k)
depth
r
, we know from
[
MP94
]
that
\exists\>x\in\mathcal{B}(G,k)
such that
V^{G_{x,r+}}\neq 0
. Since the action of
G(k)
on chambers is transitive and
r\in\frac{1}{m}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, without loss of generality we can assume that
x\in\bar{\mathcal{C}}
and
\exists\>\sigma\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{m}]
such that
V^{G_{\sigma,r+}}\neq 0
.

Theorem 5.7
.

There is an algebra homomorphism

\xi^{r}:\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]\longrightarrow\mathcal{Z}^{r}(G)

such that for any representation
(\pi,V)
of depth
r
and
0\neq v\in V^{G_{\sigma,r+}}
,
\sigma\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{m}]
, we have

\xi^{r}(\lambda)(v)=\mu(G_{\sigma,r+})\sum_{X\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}i_{\sigma,r}(\lambda)(X)\pi^{G_{\sigma,r+}}(X)v

(5.0.3)

where
i_{\sigma,r}:\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]\rightarrow C^{st}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})
is the map defined in Lemma
5.3
and
\pi^{G_{\sigma,r+}}
denotes the natural representation of
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}
on
V^{G_{\sigma,r+}}
. If
(\pi,V)
is a smooth irreducible representation of depth
<r
and
0\neq v\in V^{G_{\sigma,r}}
, then
\xi^{r}(\lambda)(v)=\lambda(\bar{0})v
.

Proof.

Let
\sigma,\tau\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{m}]
such that
\sigma\preceq\tau
. Combining (
5.0.1
) and (
5.0.2
), we see that the following diagram commutes.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\sigma,r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\tau,r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{r}_{\sigma,\tau}}

Let
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{m}]
and
n\in N_{G}(T)(k)
such that
n\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}
and
n\sigma_{1}\preceq\sigma_{2}
. Then, Lemma
5.6
gives us

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\sigma_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\sigma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\sigma_{1},r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\sigma_{2},r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{r}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},n}}

Thus, we have a map from
\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]
to the inverse system
\{\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{m}]}
, and hence a map
\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]\rightarrow\lim_{\sigma\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{m}]}\mathcal{M}^{r}_{\sigma}=A^{r}(G)\cong\mathcal{Z}^{r}(G)
. We define this map to be
\xi^{r}
. Then,

\displaystyle\xi^{r}(\lambda)(v)

\displaystyle=\xi^{r}(\lambda)(\delta_{G_{\sigma,r+}}(v))=\xi^{r}(\lambda)(\delta_{G_{\sigma,r+}})(v)=j_{\sigma,r}(\lambda)(v)=\int_{G(k)}j_{\sigma,r}(\lambda)(x)\pi(x)v\;d\mu(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{G_{\sigma,r}}(\iota_{\sigma,r}\circ i_{\sigma,r}(\lambda))(x)\pi(x)v\;d\mu(x)=\mu(G_{\sigma,r+})\sum_{\bar{x}\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}i_{\sigma,r}(\lambda)(\bar{x})\pi^{G_{\sigma,r+}}(\bar{x})v

where
\bar{x}
is the image of
x\in G_{\sigma,r}
under the projection
G_{\sigma,r}\rightarrow G_{\sigma,r}/G_{\sigma,r+}\cong\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}
. Now, if
(\pi,V)
has depth
<r
, following the same steps, we have

\xi^{r}(\lambda)(v)=\mu(G_{\sigma,r+})\sum_{\bar{x}\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}i_{\sigma,r}(\lambda)(\bar{x})v

Let
\mathbbm{1}\in C(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})
denote the function which takes the value
1
at all points. Then,

\displaystyle\mu(G_{\sigma,r+})\sum_{\bar{x}\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}i_{\sigma,r}(\lambda)(\bar{x})

\displaystyle=\mu(G_{\sigma,r+})|\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}|\left(i_{\sigma,r}(\lambda),\mathbbm{1}\right)=\mu(G_{\sigma,r+})|\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}|\left(\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}(i_{\sigma,r}(\lambda)),\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}}(\mathbbm{1})\right)

\displaystyle=\mu(G_{\sigma,r+})|\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}|\left((c^{\mu}_{\sigma,r})^{-1}\chi_{\sigma,r}^{*}(t^{*}_{\sigma}(\lambda)),|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}|^{1/2}\mathbbm{1}_{0}\right)=|\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}|\left(\chi_{\sigma,r}^{*}(t^{*}_{\sigma}(\lambda)),\mathbbm{1}_{0}\right)

\displaystyle=\chi_{\sigma,r}^{*}(t^{*}_{\sigma}(\lambda))(0)=\lambda(t_{\sigma}\circ\chi_{\sigma,r}(0))=\lambda(\bar{0}).

∎

Remark 5.8
.

Observe that if
r=\frac{i}{m}
, the map
\xi^{r}
depends on the choice of
\gamma^{i}\in\mathfrak{O}_{E}
, since
\rho_{\sigma,r}
depends on
\gamma^{i}
. Recall the diagram
4.3.12
which forms the basis of our construction.

(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}^{F}_{\sigma,r})^{*}
((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})^{F}_{\sigma,r})^{*}
(\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{F})^{*}
(\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{*}//\mathbb{L}_{\sigma})^{F}
((\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r})^{*}//\mathbb{G}_{\sigma})^{F}
(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma})^{F}
\simeq
\simeq

Note that
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{*}
,
\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}
and
\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma}
have natural
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-structure since
T
splits over
k
. The
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-structure on
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{\sigma,r}^{*}
,
\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{*}
,
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}
and
\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{*}//\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}
depends on the choice of
\gamma\in\mathfrak{O}_{E}
, while the isomorphism
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{\sigma,r}^{*}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{*}
depends only on the choice of
\gamma^{i}
. However, if we have a different
\gamma^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{O}_{E}
with
(\gamma^{\prime})^{i}=\gamma^{i}
, the diagram still holds true for the different
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-structure on
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{\sigma,r}^{*}
,
\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{*}
,
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}
and
\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{*}//\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}
and the same
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-structure
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{*}
,
\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}
and
\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W_{\sigma}
. Also, the isomorphism
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{E})_{\sigma,r}^{*}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\bar{\mathfrak{l}}_{\sigma}^{*}
still remains the same and is defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, and we have a map of Frobenius fixed points, with the Frobenius now attached to the new
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-structure. Thus, the map
\xi^{r}
depends only on
\gamma^{i}
, and we don’t need to “remember” the
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-structure induced by
\gamma
. More generally, the isomorphism and hence
\xi^{r}
only depends on a choice of
\nu\in k^{t}
such that
\mathrm{v}(\nu)=r
.

6
Parameters attached to smooth irreducible representations of positive depth

We use the maps
\xi^{r}
constructed in the previous section to attach parameters to smooth irreducible representations of depth
r
. We briefly describe restricted Langlands parameters attached to Moy-Prasad types, following
[
CDT25
]
, and show that the parameters we attached to smooth irreducible representations are same as the ones described in the afore-mentioned work.

6.1
Depth-r Deligne Lusztig parameters

Definition 6.1
.

For
c\in\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, we denote by
j_{c}:\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W\xlongrightarrow{\sim}\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W
the isomorphism induced by
\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}\xrightarrow{\times c}\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}
. The depth-
r
Deligne-Lusztig parameters of
G(k)
are defined to be the set

\text{DL}_{r}:=\{(\nu,\theta_{\nu})\>|\>\nu\in k^{t},\>\mathrm{v}(\nu)=r,\>\theta_{\nu}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q})\}/\sim

where
(\nu_{1},\theta_{\nu_{1}})\sim(\nu_{2},\theta_{\nu_{2}})
if
\theta_{\nu_{1}}=j_{c}(\theta_{\nu_{2}})
for
c=\nu_{1}/\nu_{2}+\mathfrak{m}_{k^{t}}\in\kappa_{k^{t}}=\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
.

Let
(\pi,V)
be a smooth irreducible representation of depth
r>0
, where
r=\frac{i}{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
. Let
\nu=\gamma^{i}
, where
\gamma\in\mathfrak{O}_{E}
is as described earlier. Then,
z\in\mathcal{Z}^{r}(G)
acts on
\pi
via a constant, and composing
\xi^{r}
and the evaluation map
\mathcal{Z}^{r}(G)\rightarrow\text{End}(\pi)=\mathbb{C}
, we obtain a map

\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]\xrightarrow{\xi^{r}}\mathcal{Z}^{r}(G)\rightarrow\text{End}(\pi)=\mathbb{C}

and hence an element of
(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}
, say
\tilde{\theta}_{\nu}(\pi)
given by
\xi^{r}(\lambda)v=\lambda(\tilde{\theta}_{\nu}(\pi))v
for each
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]
. Note that
\tilde{\theta}_{\nu}
gives a map
\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{r}\rightarrow(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}
which depends on the choice of
\nu
.

Thus, to each smooth irreducible representation of depth
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
, we attach can attach a depth-
r
Deligne-Lusztig parameter
\Theta_{r}(\pi)\in\mathrm{DL}_{r}
given by the equivalence class of
(\nu,\tilde{\theta}_{\nu}(\pi))
,with
\nu
chosen as described earlier and we have a map
\Theta_{r}:\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{r}\rightarrow\mathrm{DL}_{r}
defined by this assignment. Further, we observe from our construction that
\tilde{\theta}_{\nu}(\pi)\in(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)(\mathbb{F}_{q})\subset(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q})
, which happens in this case because our group is
k
-split.

Remark 6.2
.

When we attach a depth-
r
Deligne-Lusztig parameter to
\pi\in\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{r}
, the element
\nu\in k^{t}
is not an arbitrary element in
k^{t}
with
\mathrm{v}(\nu)=r
. We have chosen it such that it lies in a finite tamely ramified extension
E^{\prime}
with
e(E^{\prime}/k)=m
. Such an extension is not unique, and more generally it lies in a finite tamely ramified Galois extension
M
of
k
such that
e(M/k)\cdot r\in\mathbb{Z}
.

6.2
Deligne-Lusztig parameters attached to Moy-Prasad types

Recall the definition of a Moy-Prasad type of depth
r
for
r\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
.

Definition 6.3
.

For
r\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
, a Moy-Prasad type of depth
r
for
G(k)
is a pair
(x,\mathfrak{X})
where
x\in\mathcal{B}(G,k)
and
\mathfrak{X}\in\mathfrak{g}^{*}(k)_{x,-r}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(k)_{x,-r+}\cong(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{x,r}^{F})^{*}
. Let
\mathrm{MP}(r)
denote the set of Moy-Prasad types of depth
r
.

A pair
(x,\mathfrak{X})\in\mathrm{MP}(r)
is called non-degenerate if the coset
\mathfrak{X}=X^{*}+\mathfrak{g}^{*}(k)_{x,-r+}
representing
\mathfrak{X}
does not contain any nilpotent elements (Check
[
CDT25
]
Section 4.2.1 or
[
MP94
]
Section 3.5 for the definition of nilpotent). Two Moy-Prasad types of positive depth
(x,\mathfrak{X})
and
(y,\mathfrak{Y})
are said to be associates if they have the same depth
r
and

\mathrm{Ad}^{*}(G(k))(X^{*}+\mathfrak{g}^{*}(k)_{x,-r+})\cap\mathrm{Ad}^{*}(G(k))(Y^{*}+\mathfrak{g}^{*}(k)_{x,-r+})\neq\emptyset

where
X^{*}+\mathfrak{g}^{*}(k)_{x,-r+}
(resp.
Y^{*}+\mathfrak{g}^{*}(k)_{x,-r+}
) is the coset realizing
\mathfrak{X}
(resp.
\mathfrak{Y}
).

Remark 6.4
.

A Moy-Prasad type of depth
r
is essentially the same as an unrefined Minimal
K
-type of depth
r
, as defined in
[
MP94
]
, Section 5. For
r>0
, the character
\chi
in the definition of a minimal
K
-type can be identified with an element of
\mathfrak{X}\in\mathfrak{g}^{*}(k)_{x,-r}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(k)_{x,-r+}\cong(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{x,r}^{F})^{*}
using the fixed additive character
\tilde{\psi}:\mathbb{F}_{q}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. The notions of non-degenerate and associates are also exactly the same.

A smooth irreducible representation
(\pi,V)
of depth
r
is said to contain
(x,\mathfrak{X})\in\mathrm{MP}(r)
as a Moy-Prasad type if the natural representation
\pi^{G_{x,r+}}
of
G_{x,r}/G_{x,r+}\cong\mathfrak{g}(k)_{x,r}/\mathfrak{g}(k)_{x,r+}
on
V^{G_{x,r+}}
contains
\tilde{\psi}\circ\mathfrak{X}
as a one-dimensional sub-representation. By
[
MP94
, Theorem 5.2]
, we know that any smooth irreducible representation contains a non-degenerate Moy-Prasad type of the same depth. Further, any two Moy-Prasad types contained in
\pi
are associates of each other.

We describe how to attach Deligne-Lusztig parameters to Moy-Prasad types, restricting to the case where
G
is split over tamely ramified extension. Just for the next few paragraphs, assume that
G
is not necessarily
k
-split, but split over a tamely ramified extension and let
T
be a maximal
k
torus such that the maximal
k
-split subtorus in
T
is a maximal
k
-split torus, and the maximal
K
-split subtorus in
T
is a maximal
K
-split torus. We keep the same condition on the depth
r
, with
r\in\frac{1}{m}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\subset\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
. Let
(x,\mathfrak{X})\in\mathrm{MP}(r)
be a Moy-Prasad type of depth
r
, and
M
be a finite Galois extension of
k
such that
r\cdot e(M/k)\in\mathbb{Z}
and
G
splits over
M
. Let
\nu\in M
be such that
\mathrm{v}(\nu)=r
. The pair
(\nu,M)
is
(x,\mathfrak{X})
-adapted as per Definition 22 in
[
CDT25
, Section 4.1]
. For
x\in\mathcal{B}(G,k)
, choose
h\in G(k)
such that
hx\in\mathscr{A}=\mathcal{A}_{T}
and let
W^{M}_{hx}:=N_{(\mathbb{G}_{M^{u}})_{hx}}(\mathbb{T})/\mathbb{T}
. Consider the following commutative diagram :

\mathfrak{g}^{*}(k)_{x,-r}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(k)_{x,-r+}
\mathfrak{g}^{*}(k)_{hx,-r}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(k)_{hx,-r+}
\mathfrak{g}^{*}(M)_{hx,-r}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(M)_{hx,-r+}
\mathfrak{g}^{*}(M^{u})_{hx,-r}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(M^{u})_{hx,-r+}
\mathfrak{t}^{*}(M^{u})_{-r}/\mathfrak{t}^{*}(M^{u})_{-r+}
\mathfrak{g}^{*}(M^{u})_{hx,0}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(M^{u})_{hx,0+}
\mathfrak{t}^{*}(M^{u})_{0}/\mathfrak{t}^{*}(M^{u})_{0+}=\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{M^{u}})^{*}_{hx,0}//(\mathbb{G}_{M^{u}})_{hx,0}
\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W^{M}_{hx}
\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W
\mathrm{Ad}^{*}(h)
\times\nu
\times\nu
\sim

(6.2.1)

where the
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-varieties are identified with the set of
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-points, and the isomorphism is also an isomorphism of
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-varieties, as encountered in earlier sections. Let
i_{M,h,\nu,x}:\mathfrak{g}^{*}(k)_{x,-r}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(k)_{x,-r+}\rightarrow\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q})
be the composition (through the inverse of the isomorphism). Lemmas 23 and 24 in
[
CDT25
]
show that the map is independent of the choice of
h
and and
M
, and hence we can denote it by
i_{\nu,x}
. The depth-
r
Deligne-Lusztig parameter attached to
(x,\mathfrak{X})
is defined to be the equivalence class of
(\nu,i_{\nu,x}(\mathfrak{X}))
, and denoted by
\iota_{x}(\mathfrak{X})
.

Let
\mathrm{Ad}^{*}(h)\mathfrak{X}
be denoted by
\prescript{h}{}{}\mathfrak{X}
, and note that if
hx\in\mathscr{A}
,
i_{\nu,x}(\mathfrak{X})=i_{\nu,hx}(\prescript{h}{}{}\mathfrak{X})
and hence
\iota_{x}(\mathfrak{X})=\iota_{hx}(\prescript{h}{}{}\mathfrak{X})
, since the first step of the construction is not needed and all the other steps are exactly same. Further, since
i_{\nu,x}
does not depend on choice of
h
, we can assume without loss of generality that
hx\in\bar{\mathcal{C}}
.

Let
\mathscr{K}
be the splitting field of
G
. It is a tamely ramified Galois extension of
k
and let
e(\mathscr{K}/k)=n
be its ramification degree. Let
m_{1}=gcd(n,m)
,
n=n^{\prime}m_{1}
and
m=m_{1}m_{2}
. Let
\mathscr{E}_{m_{2}}
be a totally tamely ramified extension of
\mathscr{K}
and
\mathscr{K}_{f}
be the unique unramified extension of
\mathscr{K}
of degree
f
, such that
\mathscr{K}_{f}=\mathscr{K}(\zeta_{m_{2}})
. The number
f
is the smallest number such that
|\kappa_{\mathscr{K}}|^{f}\equiv 1(\mathrm{mod}\>m_{2})
, since
gcd(m_{2},p)=1
(Check for example
[
Neu13
, Chapter II, Prop. 7.12]
). Since the construction of
i_{\nu,x}(\mathfrak{X})
does not depend on the choice of the Galois extension
M
, we can let
M=\mathscr{E}_{m_{2}}\mathscr{K}_{f}
. Note that it is a Galois extension of
\mathscr{K}
and hence
k
, and satisfies the required properties since
e(M/k)=nm_{2}=n^{\prime}m
. Also observe that
M^{u}=\mathscr{E}_{m_{2}}^{u}=:\mathscr{M}
(say). We have a tower of local fields
k\subset\mathscr{K}\subset\mathscr{E}_{m_{2}}\subset M
, each tamely ramified over
k
. Let
\varpi_{1}\in\mathfrak{O}_{\mathscr{K}}
be an uniformizer and choose
\gamma_{1}\in\mathfrak{O}_{\mathscr{E}_{m_{2}}}
such that
\gamma_{1}^{m_{2}}=\varpi_{1}
and
\mathscr{E}_{m_{2}}=\mathscr{K}(\gamma_{1})
. Note that
\mathrm{v}(\gamma_{1})=1/nm_{2}
and
\gamma_{1}
is also an uniformizer of
\mathfrak{O}_{M}
.

Let’s follow the maps in the construction of
\iota_{x}(\mathfrak{X})
, using ideas in Section
4.3
. We can assume
x\in\mathscr{A}
. Since
T
splits over
\mathscr{K}
, we can identify
\mathbb{T}
with the reductive quotient of
T(\mathscr{M})
. Further, observe that
\mathbb{T}
is
\kappa_{\mathscr{K}}
-split and has a natural
\kappa_{\mathscr{K}}
-structure given by
\mathbb{T}(\kappa_{\mathscr{K}})=T(\mathscr{K})_{0}/T(\mathscr{K})_{0+}
. Similar statements holds true for
\bar{\mathfrak{t}}
and
\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}
. Let
\mathfrak{F}
denote the Frobenius element in
\Gal(\mathscr{K}^{u}/\mathscr{K})
, and
\mathfrak{F}_{\gamma_{1}}
be the unique Frobenius element in
\Gal(\mathscr{M}/\mathscr{K})
such that
\mathfrak{F}_{\gamma_{1}}(\gamma_{1})=\gamma_{1}
. Then
\mathfrak{F}_{\gamma_{1}}
is the topological generator of
\Gal(\mathscr{M}/\mathscr{E}_{m_{2}})
, and similar to the paragraphs just before Lemma
4.3.12
and its proof, we can give a
\kappa_{\mathscr{K}}
-structure to
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathscr{M}})^{*}_{x,r}
using
\mathfrak{F}_{\gamma_{1}}
, with
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathscr{M}})^{*}_{x,r}(\kappa_{\mathscr{K}})=\mathfrak{g}^{*}(\mathscr{E}_{m_{2}})_{x,-r}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(\mathscr{E}_{m_{2}})_{x,-r+}
, since
\kappa_{\mathscr{K}}=\kappa_{\mathscr{E}_{m_{2}}}=\kappa_{M}
.

Choose
\nu=\gamma_{1}^{in^{\prime}}
. If we look at the morphisms in (
6.2.1
), note that
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathscr{M}})^{*}_{x,r}\xlongrightarrow{\gamma_{1}^{in^{\prime}}}(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathscr{M}})^{*}_{x,0}
is defined over
\kappa_{\mathscr{K}}
since
\gamma_{1}\in\mathscr{E}_{m_{2}}
and we have a map of
\kappa_{\mathscr{K}}
points

\mathfrak{g}^{*}(\mathscr{E}_{m_{2}})_{x,-r}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(\mathscr{E}_{m_{2}})_{x,-r+}\xlongrightarrow{\gamma_{1}^{in^{\prime}}}\mathfrak{g}^{*}(\mathscr{E}_{m_{2}})_{x,0}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(\mathscr{E}_{m_{2}})_{x,0+}=(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathscr{M}})^{*}_{x,0}(\kappa_{\mathscr{K}}).

Similarly, the map
\bar{\mathfrak{t}}_{r}^{*}\rightarrow\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}
is also defined over
\kappa_{\mathscr{K}}
, and we have a map of
\kappa_{\mathscr{K}}
-points with
\mathfrak{t}^{*}(\mathscr{E}_{m_{2}})_{0}/\mathfrak{t}^{*}(\mathscr{E}_{m_{2}})_{0+}=\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}(\kappa_{\mathscr{K}})
. The isomorphism
\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W^{\mathscr{M}}_{x}\rightarrow(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathscr{M}})^{*}_{x,0}//(\mathbb{G}_{\mathscr{M}})_{x,0}
is induced by
\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}\rightarrow(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathscr{M}})^{*}_{x,0}
, and is defined over
\kappa_{\mathscr{K}}
since
\mathbb{T}
is
\mathscr{K}
-split. Using the above facts, we see that the maps between
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
varieties and vector spaces in (
6.2.1
) are defined over
\kappa_{\mathscr{K}}
, and we have a diagram of
\kappa_{\mathscr{K}}
-points.

\mathfrak{g}^{*}(k)_{x,-r}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(k)_{x,-r+}
\mathfrak{g}^{*}(\mathscr{K})_{x,-r}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(\mathscr{K})_{x,-r+}
\mathfrak{g}^{*}(\mathscr{E}_{m_{2}})_{x,-r}/\mathfrak{g}^{*}(\mathscr{E}_{m_{2}})_{x,-r+}
\mathfrak{t}^{*}(\mathscr{E}_{m_{2}})_{-r}/\mathfrak{t}^{*}(\mathscr{E}_{m_{2}})_{-r+}
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathscr{M}})^{*}_{x,0}(\kappa_{\mathscr{K}})
\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}(\kappa_{\mathscr{K}})
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathscr{M}})^{*}_{x,0}//(\mathbb{G}_{\mathscr{M}})_{hx,0}(\kappa_{\mathscr{K}})
\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W^{M}_{x}(\kappa_{\mathscr{K}})
\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W(\kappa_{\mathscr{K}})
\times\nu
\times\nu
\sim

(6.2.2)

From the commutative diagram (
6.2.2
), we can conclude that
i_{\nu,x}(\mathfrak{X})
lies in
\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W(\kappa_{\mathscr{K}})
, where
\mathscr{K}
is the splitting field of
G
. Similar to Remark
5.8
, we have a natural
\kappa_{\mathscr{K}}
-structure on
\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}
and
\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W
, while the
\kappa_{\mathscr{K}}
-structure on
(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathscr{M}})^{*}_{x,0}
depends on choice of
\gamma_{1}
. However, if we choose some other
\gamma_{1}^{\prime}\neq\gamma_{1}
, we will have
(\nu=\gamma_{1}^{in^{\prime}},i_{\nu,x}(\mathfrak{X}))\sim(\nu^{\prime}=(\gamma^{\prime}_{1})^{in^{\prime}},i_{\nu^{\prime},x}(\mathfrak{X}))
.

Now, if we return to the setting where
G
is
k
-split, we have
k=\mathscr{K}
and
\kappa_{\mathscr{K}}=\mathbb{F}_{q}
. So,
i_{\nu,x}(\mathfrak{X})\in\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W(\mathbb{F}_{q})
, similar to the case of
\theta_{\nu}(\pi)
for a smooth irreducible representation
\pi
. In fact, as shown in Proposition
6.5
, if
(x,\mathfrak{X})
is a Moy-Prasad type of depth
r
contained in
\pi\in\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{r}
, then
\Theta_{r}(\pi)=\iota_{x}(\mathfrak{X})
. Further, since
r=i/m
, we can replace
x
with
\sigma\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{m}]
such that
x\in\sigma
(WLOG, we can assume
x\in\bar{\mathcal{C}})
, and we observe that
i_{\nu,x}(\mathfrak{X})=t_{\sigma}\circ\chi_{\sigma,r}(\mathfrak{X})
.

Proposition 6.5
.

Let
(\pi,V)
be a smooth irreducible representation of depth
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
, and let
(x,\mathfrak{X})
be a Moy-Prasad type contained in
(\pi,V)
. Then
\iota_{x}(\mathfrak{X})=\Theta_{r}(\pi)
.

Proof.

Let
\iota_{x}(\mathfrak{X})=\overline{(\nu^{\prime},i_{\nu^{\prime},x}(\mathfrak{X})}
and
\Theta_{r}(\pi)=\overline{(\nu,\tilde{\theta}_{\nu}(\pi))}
. Without loss of generality, we can assume that
x\in\bar{\mathcal{C}}
and
\nu^{\prime}=\nu
. Let
r=\frac{i}{m}
with
p\nmid m
. Then, we can pick
\sigma\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{m}]
such that
x\in\sigma
and
G_{x,r+}=G_{\sigma,r+}
. Pick
v\in V^{G_{\sigma,r+}}
such that
\pi^{G_{\sigma,r+}}(X)v=\tilde{\psi}(\mathfrak{X}(X))v
for
X\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}
, where
\pi^{G_{\sigma,r+}}
is as defined in Theorem
5.7
. For any
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]
, we have

\lambda(\tilde{\theta}_{\nu}(\pi))v=\xi^{r}(\lambda)v=\mu(G_{\sigma,r+})\sum_{X\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}i_{\sigma,r}(\lambda)(X)\pi^{G_{\sigma,r+}}(X)v=\mu(G_{\sigma,r+})\sum_{X\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}i_{\sigma,r}(\lambda)(X)\tilde{\psi}(\mathfrak{X}(X))v

Using Proposition
4.4

(i)
, we see that

\mu(G_{\sigma,r+})\sum_{X\in\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}i_{\sigma,r}(\lambda)(X)\tilde{\psi}(\mathfrak{X}(X))=\mu(G_{\sigma,r+})\sum_{X^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(i_{\sigma,r}(\lambda))(X^{*})\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(\tilde{\psi}\circ(-\mathfrak{X}))(X^{*})

From a simple calculation, it follows that

\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(\tilde{\psi}\circ(-\mathfrak{X}))=\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right|^{1/2}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{X}}

and using definition of
i_{\sigma,r}
, we have

\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(i_{\sigma,r}(\lambda))=(c^{\mu}_{\sigma,r})^{-1}\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(\rho_{\sigma,r}\circ t^{*}_{\sigma}(\lambda))=(c^{\mu}_{\sigma,r})^{-1}\chi_{\sigma,r}^{*}\circ t^{*}_{\sigma}(\lambda)

Using the above results, we note that for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]

\displaystyle\lambda(\tilde{\theta}_{\nu}(\pi))

\displaystyle=\mu(G_{\sigma,r+})\sum_{X^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(i_{\sigma,r}(\lambda))(X^{*})\mathcal{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}}(\tilde{\psi}\circ(-\mathfrak{X}))(X^{*})

\displaystyle=\mu(G_{\sigma,r+})\cdot\left|\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F}\right|^{1/2}\cdot(c^{\mu}_{\sigma,r})^{-1}\sum_{X^{*}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma,r}^{F})^{*}}\chi_{\sigma,r}^{*}\circ t^{*}_{\sigma}(\lambda)(X^{*})\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{X}}(X^{*})

\displaystyle=\chi_{\sigma,r}^{*}\circ t^{*}_{\sigma}(\lambda)(\mathfrak{X})=\lambda(i_{\nu,x}(\mathfrak{X}))

and hence
\Theta_{r}(\pi)=\iota_{x}(\mathfrak{X})
.
∎

Remark 6.6
.

Two Moy-Prasad types are defined to be stable associates if they have the same Deligne-Lusztig parameter attached to it. In
[
CDT25
, Lemma 36]
, it is proved that two Moy-Prasad types which are associates of each other are stable associates. Since Moy-Prasad types contained in a smooth irreducible representation are associates of each other, this essentially attaches a Deligne-Lusztig parameter
\iota_{\pi}
to
\pi
. The previous proposition gives an alternative proof of the fact that any two Moy-Prasad types contained in a smooth irreducible representation have the same Deligne-Lusztig parameter attached to it, and it is shown to be equal to the parameter
\Theta_{r}(\pi)
attached to
\pi\in\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{r}
in Section
6.1
. Further,
[
CDT25
, Lemma 33]
shows that if the depth
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
,
\iota_{x}(\mathfrak{X})
is non-zero if and only if
(x,\mathfrak{X})
is non-degenerate. Since every smooth irreducible representation contains a non-degenerate Moy-Prasad type, we have
\iota_{\pi}=\Theta_{r}(\pi)
and
\Theta_{r}(\pi)
is non-trivial for any
\pi\in\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{r}
with
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
.
Let
\bar{0}
denote the image of
0
in
(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}
and
\mathrm{DL}_{r}^{\circ}
denote the subset of
\mathrm{DL}_{r}
containing non-trivial depth-
r
Deligne-Lusztig parameters, i.e.,
\overline{(\nu_{r},\theta_{\nu_{r}})}
such that
\theta_{\nu_{r}}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}\setminus\{\bar{0}\}
. Then,
\Theta_{r}
is basically a map
\Theta_{r}:\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{r}\rightarrow\mathrm{DL}_{r}^{\circ}
for
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
.

6.3
Restricted depth-
r
parameters

Let
G^{\vee}
denote the dual connected reductive group over
\mathbb{C}
, and
T^{\vee}
the dual complex torus. Let
W_{k}
denote the absolute Weil group of
k
( see
[
Mez09
, Section 6.3]
), and
I_{k}=\Gal(\bar{k}/K)
denote the Inertia subgroup of the Weil group. We have the usual upper numbering filtration of the Galois group
\Gamma_{k}=\Gal(\bar{k}/k)
denoted by
\Gamma^{r}_{k}
(see
[
Ser79
, Section 3 of Chapter IV]
). For infinite extensions,
\Gamma_{k}^{r}
is defined as the inverse limit
\varprojlim\Gal(L/k)
for finite Galois extensions of
k
. Similarly, we have the upper numbering filtrations of the Weil group, denoted
\{I_{k}^{r}\;|\>r\geq 0\}
, where
I_{k}^{0}=I_{k}
is the inertia subgroup and
I_{k}^{0+}
is the wild inertia subgroup. Let
I_{k}^{r+}
denote the closure of
\cup_{s>r}I_{k}^{s}
. The group
I^{r}_{k}
has the subspace topology from
I_{k}
, and we equip
I_{k}^{r}/I_{k}^{r+}
with the quotient topology.

Definition 6.7
.

Let
r\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
. A continuous homomorphism
\varphi:I_{k}^{r}/I_{k}^{r+}\rightarrow G^{\vee}
, is a tame restricted depth-
r
parameter if there exists a maximal torus
T^{\vee}\subset G^{\vee}
and a continuous homomorphism
\tilde{\varphi}:I_{k}^{0+}\rightarrow T^{\vee}
, trivial on
I_{k}^{r+}
such that
\tilde{\varphi}|_{I_{k}^{r}}\equiv\varphi
. Let
\mathrm{RP}_{r}
denote the set of
G^{\vee}
-conjugacy classes of tame restricted depth-
r
parameters.

Let
\pi\in\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{r}
such that
\Theta_{r}(\pi)=\overline{(\nu,\tilde{\theta}_{\nu}(\pi))}\in\mathrm{DL}_{r}
for
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
. Let
M
denote a finite tamely ramified Galois extension of
k
such that
r\cdot e(M/k)\in\mathbb{Z}
. Given the fixed additive character
\tilde{\psi}:\kappa_{k}=\mathbb{F}_{q}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, we have an additive character
\tilde{\psi}_{M}:\kappa_{M}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
given by
\tilde{\psi}_{M}(x)=\tilde{\psi}(\mathrm{Tr}_{\kappa_{M}/\mathbb{F}_{q}}(x))
. The map
\mathrm{p}:\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}\rightarrow\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W
is surjective at the level of
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-points. For
\tilde{\theta}_{\nu}(\pi)\in\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W(\mathbb{F}_{q})
, there exists a finite extension
\mathbbm{k}
over
k
and
X\in\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}(\mathbbm{k})
such that
X
maps to
\tilde{\theta}_{\nu}(\pi)
under the map
\mathrm{p}
. Thus, possibly replacing
M
with a finite unramified extension of itself, we have
X\in\mathfrak{t}^{*}(M)_{-r}/\mathfrak{t}^{*}(M)_{-r+}\cong\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}(\kappa_{M})
such that
X
maps to
\tilde{\theta}_{\nu}(\pi)
under the map

\mathfrak{t}^{*}(M)_{-r}/\mathfrak{t}^{*}(M)_{-r+}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}(\kappa_{M})\rightarrow(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)(\kappa_{M})

Given such an
X
, we can define an additive character on
\mathfrak{t}(M)_{r}/\mathfrak{t}(M)_{r+}
in the following way-

\displaystyle\mathfrak{t}(M)_{r}/\mathfrak{t}(M)_{r+}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}

\displaystyle Y\>\>

\displaystyle\longmapsto\tilde{\psi}_{M}(X(Y))

Using the Moy-Prasad isomorphism
T(M)_{r}/T(M)_{r+}\cong\mathfrak{t}(M)_{r}/\mathfrak{t}(M)_{r+}
, we can pull it back to a character of
T(M)_{r}/T(M)_{r+}
and hence a character of
T(M)_{r}
denoted by
\chi_{X,M}
via pullback along the quotient. Let
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
are two characters of
T(M)
whose restriction to
T(M)_{r}
gives
\chi_{X,M}
, and denote their associated Langlands parameters via Local Langlands for Tori (see
[
Yu09a
, Section7.5]
) by
\varphi_{i}:W_{M}\rightarrow T^{\vee}
,
i=1,2
and they clearly have depth
\leq r
. Since
\psi_{1}^{-1}\psi_{2}
has depth less than
r
, by depth preservation for tamely ramified tori (see
[
Yu09a
, Section 7.10]
)
\varphi_{1}^{-1}\varphi_{2}
will also have depth less than
r
, and hence
\varphi_{1}
and
\varphi_{2}
have the same restriction to
I_{M}^{r}/I_{M}^{r+}
. Further, since
r>0
and
M/k
is tamely ramified, we have that
I_{M}^{r}=I_{M}^{r}\cap\Gal(\bar{k}/M)^{r}=I^{r}_{M}\cap\Gal(\bar{k}/M^{t})=I^{r}_{M}\cap\Gal(\bar{k}/k^{t})=I_{k}^{r}
. Hence, to
\overline{(\nu,\tilde{\theta}_{\nu}(\pi))}
, we can attach a continuous homomorphism
\varphi^{T}_{X,M}:I_{k}^{r}/I_{k}^{r+}\rightarrow T^{\vee}
. We have an embedding
T^{\vee}\hookrightarrow G^{\vee}
determined upto
G^{\vee}
conjugation, and composing with that,
\varphi_{X,M}
gives a tame restricted depth-
r
parameter.

Observe that the process of attaching restricted depth-
r
parameter
\varphi^{T}_{X,M}
to
\Theta_{r}(\pi)
is exactly the same as in
[
CDT25
]
, combining the steps mentioned in Section 3.2 and the proof of Lemma 40. Thus,
\varphi^{T}_{X,M}
is the same as
\phi^{T}_{X,M}
as defined in
[
CDT25
, Section 5.1]
and using
[
CDT25
, Corollary 19]
, we can conclude that
\varphi^{T}_{X,M}
is independent of the choice of
M
. Hence, we can denote it by
\varphi^{T}_{X}
. Further, the
G^{\vee}
-conjugacy class of
\varphi_{X}^{T}
depends only on
\Theta_{r}(\pi)
(check
[
CDT25
, Lemma 41]
, and hence gives an unique element
\varphi_{\iota_{\pi}}\in\mathrm{RP}_{r}
. There is in fact a bijection between
\mathrm{DL}_{r}
and
\mathrm{RP}_{r}
, as shown in
[
CDT25
, Lemma 45]
. Thus for
r\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}\cap\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
, we have a map

\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{r}\xrightarrow{\Theta_{r}}\mathrm{DL}_{r}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{RP}_{r}

and each element in the image of this map is non-trivial when
\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{r}\neq\emptyset
.

7
The depth zero case

In this section, we will construct stable functions on the depth zero Moy-Prasad filtraion quotient, and use that to construct elements in the depth zero center. We also define and attach depth-zero Deligne Lusztig parameters to smooth irreducible representations of depth zero using the constructed elements. This is basically a generalization of the results in
[
CB24
]
to the reductive group case, and we use the complex dual torus instead of the
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-dual. Many of the proofs here can be directly lifted from
[
CB24
]
, and we will refer to it as and when it seems fit.

7.1
Stable functions on the depth-zero quotient

For
\sigma\in[\mathscr{A}]
, let
C(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F})
denote the space of class functions (conjugation invariant functions) on the depth-zero quotient
\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}=G_{\sigma,0}/G_{\sigma,0+}
, equipped with the convolution product

f*g(x)=\sum_{y\in\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}}f(xy^{-1})g(y)

For
f,g\in C(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F})
, we have the standard inner product on
C(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F})
given by

(f,g)=|\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}|^{-1}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}}f(x)\overline{g(x)}.

Note that in the depth-zero case,
\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}\cong\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}
and for
\sigma\preceq\tau\in[\mathscr{A}]
,
\mathbb{P}_{\sigma,\tau}\subset\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}
as defined in Section
4.3
is an
F
-stable parabolic subgroup with Levi decomposition
\mathbb{P}_{\sigma,\tau}\cong\mathbb{U}_{\sigma,\tau}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{\tau}
, where
\mathbb{G}_{\tau}\subset\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}
is an
F
-stable Levi subgroup. We have the parabolic restriction map
\mathrm{res}_{\mathbb{G}_{\tau}}^{\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}}:C(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F})\rightarrow C(\mathbb{G}_{\tau}^{F})

\mathrm{res}_{\mathbb{G}_{\tau}}^{\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}}(f)(l)=\sum_{u\in\mathbb{U}_{\sigma,\tau}^{F}}f(lu)

We fix an isomorphism

\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times}\simeq(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})_{p^{\prime}}

(7.1.1)

where
(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})_{p^{\prime}}
denotes the subgroup of elements in
\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}
of order prime to
p
. Let
G_{\sigma}^{\vee}
denote the complex dual of
\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}
and
T^{\vee}=X^{*}(\mathbb{T})\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\times}\cong X^{*}(T)\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\times}
denote the complex dual of
\mathbb{T}
. Identifying
X^{*}(\mathbb{T})
and
X^{*}(T)
, it can be thought of as the complex dual of
T
as well. Let
[q]
denote the morphism
x\mapsto x^{q}
on
G_{\sigma}^{\vee}
. The set of semisimple conjugacy classes in
G_{\sigma}^{\vee}
stable under
[q]
are in bijection with
(T^{\vee}//W_{\sigma})^{[q]}
. We have a surjective map

\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}:\mathrm{Irr}(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F})\rightarrow(T^{\vee}//W_{\sigma})^{[q]}

where
\mathrm{Irr}(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F})
is the set of isomorphism classes of irreducible complex representations of the finite group
\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}
. This is essentially the map described in
[
DL76
, Section 5]
, but using the complex dual as described in
[
Lus07
, Section 16]
instead of the
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-dual as in the original work. This depends on the chosen isomorphism
7.1.1
. As mentioned in
[
Lus07
]
, using the complex dual is more canonical since it involves choosing only one ismorphism as opposed to two as in
[
DL76
]
. This decomposes
\mathrm{Irr}(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F})
into packets given by
\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}^{-1}(\theta)
for
\theta\in(T^{\vee}//W_{\sigma})^{[q]}
.
For each
f\in C(\mathbb{G}^{F}_{\sigma})
and
(\pi,V)\in\mathrm{Irr}(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F})
, Schur’s lemma implies that we have a function
\mathrm{Irr}(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F})\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
such that

\sum_{g\in\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}}f(g)\pi(g)=\gamma_{f}(\pi)\mathrm{Id}_{V}

(7.1.2)

This gives a bijection between
C(\mathbb{G}^{F}_{\sigma})
and gamma functions
\gamma:\mathrm{Irr}(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F})\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
with inverse given by
\gamma\mapsto f_{\gamma}
such that

f_{\gamma}(x):=|\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}|^{-1}\sum_{\pi\in\mathrm{Irr}(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F})}\gamma(\pi)\chi_{\pi}(1)\overline{\chi_{\pi}(x)},

(7.1.3)

where
\chi_{\pi}
denotes the character of
\pi
.

Definition 7.1
.

A function
f\in C(\mathbb{G}^{F}_{\sigma})
is defined to be stable if
\gamma_{f}:\mathrm{Irr}(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F})\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
factors through
\mathrm{Irr}(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F})\rightarrow(T^{\vee}//W_{\sigma})^{[q]}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
, i.e.,
\gamma_{f}
is constant on the packets
\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}^{-1}(\theta)
and hence can be viewed as functions on the set
(T^{\vee}//W_{\sigma})^{[q]}
. We denote the space of stable functions by
C^{st}(\mathbb{G}^{F}_{\sigma})
.

Let
\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W_{\sigma})^{[q]}]
denote the space of complex functions on the set
(T^{\vee}//W_{\sigma})^{[q]}
, with multiplictaion given by the ususal pointwise multiplication of functions. For
\sigma\preceq\tau\in[\mathscr{A}]
,
W_{\tau}\subset W_{\sigma}
and we have a canonical map
T^{\vee}//W_{\tau}\rightarrow T^{\vee}//W_{\sigma}
compatible with
[q]
. We denote the map given by pullback along the natural map
(T^{\vee}//W_{\tau})^{[q]}\rightarrow(T^{\vee}//W_{\sigma})^{[q]}
by

\mathrm{Res}^{\sigma}_{\tau}:\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W_{\sigma})^{[q]}]\rightarrow\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W_{\tau})^{[q]}]

Proposition 7.2

(Properties of stable functions)
.

(1)

For
\sigma\in[\mathscr{A}]
, there is an algebra isomorphism

\rho_{\sigma,0}:\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W_{\sigma})^{[q]}]\xlongrightarrow{\simeq}C^{st}(\mathbb{G}^{F}_{\sigma})

which sends the characteristic function
\mathbbm{1}_{\theta}
of
\theta\in\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W_{\sigma})^{[q]}]
to the idempotent projector
f_{\theta}\in C^{st}(\mathbb{G}^{F}_{\sigma})
for the packet
\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}^{-1}(\theta)
with
\gamma_{f_{\theta}}(\pi)=1
if
\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}(\pi)=1
, and
\gamma_{f_{\theta}}(\pi)=0
otherwise.

(2)

For
\sigma\preceq\tau\in[\mathscr{A}]
and
f\in C^{st}(\mathbb{G}^{F}_{\sigma})
, we have
\mathrm{res}^{\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}}_{\mathbb{G}_{\tau}}\in\in C^{st}(\mathbb{G}^{F}_{\tau})
, and the following diagram commutes.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W_{\sigma})^{[q]}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\mathbb{G}^{F}_{\sigma})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W_{\tau})^{[q]}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\mathbb{G}^{F}_{\tau})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\sigma,0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}^{\sigma}_{\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{res}^{\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}}_{\mathbb{G}_{\tau}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\tau,0}}

(7.1.4)

(3)

For any
f\in C^{st}(\mathbb{G}^{F}_{\sigma})
and
\sigma\preceq\tau\in[\mathscr{A}]
, we have

\sum_{u\in\mathbb{U}_{\sigma,\tau}^{F}}f(xu)=0\text{ for }x\not\in\mathbb{P}_{\sigma,\tau}^{F}

Proof.

These are basically restatments of the results in
[
CB24
, Section 4.1]
in the current setting.

(1)

The proof follows from the definition of stable functions, (
7.1.3
) and the fact that
\gamma_{f*g}(\pi)=\gamma_{f}(\pi)\cdot\gamma_{g}(\pi)
.

(2)

This is proved in
[
LL23
, Proposition 4.2.2(4)]
.

(3)

This is
[
CB24
, Theorem 4.2]
.

∎

Proposition 7.3
.

Let
\sigma\in[\mathscr{A}]
and
n\in N_{G}(T)(k)
. The adjoint action of
n
induces an isomorphism

C^{st}(\mathbb{G}^{F}_{\sigma})\xlongrightarrow{\simeq}C^{st}(\mathbb{G}^{F}_{n\sigma})

Proof.

The adjoint action of
n
induces an isomorphism
\mathbb{T}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Ad}(n)}\mathbb{T}
, and hence an action on
X^{*}(\mathbb{T})
. Thus we get an action of
n
on
T^{\vee}=X^{*}(\mathbb{T})\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\times}
induced by the adjoint action, which gives an isomorphism
T^{\vee}\rightarrow T^{\vee}
which is equivariant with respect
W_{\sigma}
action (with
W_{\sigma}
acting on the r.h.s via the isomorphism
W_{\sigma}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Ad}(n)}W_{n\sigma}
. This gives an isomorphism
T^{\vee}//W_{\sigma}\xrightarrow[\mathrm{Ad}(n)]{\simeq}T^{\vee}//W_{n\sigma}
compatible with
[q]
. Further, the adjoint action of
n
induces an isomorphism
\mathrm{Irr}(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F})\xrightarrow[\mathrm{Ad}(n)]{\simeq}\mathrm{Irr}(\mathbb{G}_{n\sigma}^{F})
, and hence the following diagram with the natural maps commutes.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Irr}(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(T^{\vee}//W_{\sigma})^{[q]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Irr}(\mathbb{G}_{n\sigma}^{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(T^{\vee}//W_{n\sigma})^{[q]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}_{n\sigma}}

Then, the definition of stable functions and part
(1)
of the previous proposition give us the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W_{\sigma})^{[q]}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\mathbb{G}^{F}_{\sigma})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W_{n\sigma})^{[q]}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\mathbb{G}^{F}_{n\sigma})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{Ad}(n^{-1}))^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\sigma,0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ad}(n^{-1})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{n\sigma,0}}

(7.1.5)

and hence the isomorphism
C^{st}(\mathbb{G}^{F}_{\sigma})\xrightarrow{\simeq}C^{st}(\mathbb{G}^{F}_{n\sigma})
.
∎

7.2
From stable functions to depth-zero Bernstein center

Let
\sigma\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}]
. Using the embedding
W_{\sigma}\hookrightarrow W
as in Section
5
, we have a map

t_{\sigma,0}:(T^{\vee}//W_{\sigma})^{[q]}\longrightarrow(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}.

We have a natural inclusion map

\iota_{\sigma,0}:C(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F})\rightarrow C_{c}^{\infty}\left(\frac{G_{\sigma,0}/G_{\sigma,0+}}{G_{\sigma,0}}\right)\rightarrow C_{c}^{\infty}\left(\frac{G(k)/G_{\sigma,0+}}{G_{\sigma,0}}\right)=\mathcal{M}^{0}_{\sigma}

sending
C(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F})\ni f:\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
to
\iota_{\sigma,r}(f)\in\mathcal{M}^{0}_{\sigma}
supported in
G_{\sigma,0}\subset G(k)
given by

\iota_{\sigma,0}(f):G_{\sigma,0}\rightarrow G_{\sigma,0}/G_{\sigma,0+}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}\xlongrightarrow{f}\mathbb{C}

Note that
\tilde{\iota}_{\sigma,0}:=\mu(G_{\sigma,0+})^{-1}\iota_{\sigma,0}
is an algebra morphism sending the identity
\mathbbm{1}_{e}\in C(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F})
to
\delta_{G_{\sigma,0+}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\sigma}^{0}
. Consider the composed map

j_{\sigma,0}:\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}]\xrightarrow{t^{*}_{\sigma,0}}\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W_{\sigma})^{[q]}]\xrightarrow{\rho_{\sigma,0}}C^{st}(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F})\xlongrightarrow{\mu(G_{\sigma,0+})^{-1}\iota_{\sigma,0}}\mathcal{M}_{\sigma}^{0}

We will follow steps similar to Section
5
to construct a map
\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}]\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}^{0}(G)
.

Lemma 7.4
.

For
\sigma\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}]
, we have a map

i_{\sigma,0}:\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}]\longrightarrow C^{st}(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F})

such that for
\sigma\preceq\tau\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{m}]
, we have the following commutative diagram.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\mathbb{G}_{\tau}^{F})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\sigma,0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\tau,0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{res}^{\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}}_{\mathbb{G}_{\tau}}}

(7.2.1)

Proof.

For
\sigma\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}]
, define
i_{\sigma,0}:\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}]\longrightarrow C^{st}(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F})
as the composition

\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}]\xlongrightarrow{t^{*}_{\sigma,0}}\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W_{\sigma})^{[q]}]\xlongrightarrow{\rho_{\sigma,0}}C^{st}(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F})

For
\sigma\preceq\tau\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{m}]
, it immediately follows that the following diagram commutes.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W_{\sigma})^{[q]}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W_{\tau})^{[q]}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t^{*}_{\sigma,0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t^{*}_{\tau,0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}^{\sigma}_{\tau}}

Combining the above diagram and (
7.1.4
) proves the lemma.
∎

Lemma 7.5
.

For
\sigma\preceq\tau\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}]
, the map
\tilde{\iota}_{\sigma,0}=\mu(G_{\sigma,0+})^{-1}\iota_{\sigma,0}:C^{st}(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F})\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{0}_{\sigma}
fits into the following commutative diagram of algebra morphisms.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{0}_{\sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\mathbb{G}_{\tau}^{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{0}_{\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\iota}_{\sigma,0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{res}^{\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}}_{\mathbb{G}_{\tau}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{0}_{\sigma,\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\iota}_{\sigma,0}}

(7.2.2)

Proof.

This is just a rescaled version of
[
CB24
, Lemma 5.1]
.
∎

Lemma 7.6
.

Let
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}]
and
n\in N_{G}(T)(k)
such that
n\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}
and
n\sigma_{1}\preceq\sigma_{2}
. Then, we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{0}_{\sigma_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{0}_{\sigma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\sigma_{1},0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\sigma_{2},0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{0}_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},n}}

Proof.

The proof uses the same ideas as the proof of Lemma
5.6
. Using (
7.1.5
) , we have

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W_{\sigma_{1}})^{[q]}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma_{1}}^{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W_{n\sigma_{1}})^{[q]}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\mathbb{G}_{n\sigma_{1}}^{F})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t^{*}_{\sigma_{1},0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t^{*}_{n\sigma_{1},0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\sigma_{1},0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{n\sigma_{1},0}}

Then, applying Lemma
7.4
to
n\sigma_{1}\preceq\sigma_{2}
and combining with the above diagram, we have the following.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma_{1}}^{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\mathbb{G}_{n\sigma_{1}}^{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma_{2}}^{F})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\sigma_{1},0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{n\sigma_{1},0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\sigma_{2},0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{res}^{\mathbb{G}_{n\sigma_{1}}}_{\mathbb{G}_{\sigma_{2}}}}

Further, the adjoint action of
n
gives us an isomorphism
\mathrm{Ad}(n^{-1})^{*}:\mathcal{M}^{0}_{\sigma_{1}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{M}^{0}_{n\sigma_{1}}
and hence a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma_{1}}^{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{0}_{\sigma_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\mathbb{G}_{n\sigma_{1}}^{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{0}_{n\sigma_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{st}(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma_{2}}^{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{0}_{\sigma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\iota}_{\sigma_{1},0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\iota}_{n\sigma_{1},0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{res}^{\mathbb{G}_{n\sigma_{1}}}_{\mathbb{G}_{\sigma_{2}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{0}_{n\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\iota}_{\sigma_{2},0}}

Since
j_{\sigma,r}=\tilde{\iota}_{\sigma,r}\circ i_{\sigma,r}
, combining the above two diagrams proves the lemma.
∎

Theorem 7.7
.

There is an algebra homomorphism

\xi^{0}:\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Z}^{0}(G)

such that for any depth-zero representation
(\pi,V)
and
0\neq v\in V^{G_{\sigma,0+}}
,
\sigma\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}]
, we have

\xi^{0}(\lambda)(v)=\sum_{x\in\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}}i_{\sigma,0}(\lambda)(x)\pi^{G_{\sigma,0+}}(x)v

(7.2.3)

where
i_{\sigma,0}:\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}\rightarrow C^{st}(\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F})
is the map defined in Lemma
7.4
and
\pi^{G_{\sigma,0+}}
denotes the natural representation of
\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}
on
V^{G_{\sigma,0+}}
.

Proof.

Using the previous lemmas, we see that we have a map from
\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}
to
\mathcal{M}^{0}_{\sigma}
for each
\sigma\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}]
compatible with the inverse system maps
\phi^{0}_{\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}}
. Hence, we have a map
\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}\rightarrow\lim_{\sigma\in[\mathcal{C}]}\mathcal{M}^{0}_{\sigma}\cong\mathcal{Z}^{0}(G)
and we define this to be
\xi^{0}
. Then, calculations similar to Theorem
5.7
give (
7.2.3
) and finishes the proof.
∎

7.3
Depth-zero Deligne-Lusztig parameters

Let
S
be a
k
-split maximal torus of
G
. For another
k
-split maximal torus
S^{\prime}
, there exists
g\in G(k)
such that
S^{\prime}=\mathrm{Ad}_{g}(S)
, and the pulllback along the map
\mathrm{Ad}_{g}:S\rightarrow S^{\prime}
gives rise to a canonical isomorphism
(S^{\prime})^{\vee}=X^{*}(S^{\prime})\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\times}\rightarrow X^{*}(S)\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\vee}=S^{\vee}
equivariant with respect to
[q]
. Let
W_{S}=W(G,S)
denote the Weyl group of
G
with respect to
S
.

Definition 7.8
.

We define the set of depth-zero Deligne-Lusztig parameters of
G(k)
to be

\mathrm{DL}_{0}:=\lim_{S}(S^{\vee}//W_{S})^{[q]}

where the limit is over all
k
-split maximal tori.

Remark 7.9
.

Our definition is equivalent to the definition of
\mathrm{DL}_{0}
in
[
CDT25
, Section 7.1]
, when restricted to our setting. This is because we have assumed
G
to be
k
-split. Further, since we had fixed a
k
-split maximal tori
T
, without loss of generality we can represent a depth zero Deligne-Lusztig parameter by an element in
(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}
, which we will do in some cases henceforth. So, we can equivalently define the set
(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}
to be the set of depth-zero Deligne-Lusztig parameters, as done in
[
CB24
]
.

Let
(\pi,V)
be a smooth irreducible complex representation of depth zero. Then,
z\in\mathcal{Z}^{0}(G)
acts on
\pi
via a scalar in
\mathbb{C}
, and composing
\xi^{0}
with the evaluation map
\mathcal{Z}^{0}(G)\rightarrow\mathrm{End}(\pi)=\mathbb{C}
, we have a map

\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}]\xrightarrow{\xi^{0}}\mathcal{Z}^{0}(G)\rightarrow\mathrm{End}(\pi)=\mathbb{C}

and hence an element of
\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}]
, denoted
\Theta_{0}(\pi)
given by
\xi^{0}(\lambda)v=\lambda(\Theta_{0}(\pi))v
for
v\in V
and
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}]
. Thus, to each smooth irreducible representation of depth zero, we can attach a depth-zero Deligne-Lusztig parameter
\Theta_{0}(\pi)\in\mathrm{DL}_{0}
which gives a map
\Theta_{0}:\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{0}\rightarrow\mathrm{DL}_{0}
.

Combining all the maps
\Theta_{r}
for
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}
, we see that we have a map

\Theta:\coprod_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}}\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{r}\longrightarrow\coprod_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}}\mathrm{DL}_{r}

with
\Theta(\pi):=\Theta_{\rho(\pi)}(\pi)\in\mathrm{DL}_{\rho(\pi)}
for
\rho(\pi)\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}
, where
\rho(\pi)
denotes the depth of
\pi
. Further, for
\rho(\pi)\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
,
\Theta(\pi)\in\mathrm{DL}_{\rho(\pi)}^{\circ}
. Thus, we actually have a map

\Theta:\coprod_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}}\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{r}\longrightarrow\mathrm{DL}_{0}\coprod\left(\coprod_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{>0}}\mathrm{DL}_{r}^{\circ}\right)

(7.3.1)

7.4
Deligne-Lusztig prameters attached to depth-zero Moy-Prasad types

Definition 7.10
.

A depth-zero Moy-Prasad type of
G(k)
is a pair
(x,\mathcal{X})
where
x\in\mathcal{B}(G,k)
and
\mathcal{X}
is an irreducible cuspidal representation of
\mathbb{G}_{x}^{F}\cong G_{x,0}/G_{x,0+}
inflated to the parahoric subgroup
G_{x,0}
. Let
\mathrm{MP}(0)
denote the set of Moy-Prasad types of depth zero.

Two Moy-Prasad types of depth zero
(x,\mathcal{X})
and
(y,\mathcal{Y})
are said to be associates if there exists
g\in G(k)
such that
G_{x,0}\cap G_{gy,0}
surjects onto both
\mathbb{G}_{x}
and
\mathbb{G}_{gy}
and
\mathcal{X}
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{Ad}(g)\mathcal{Y}
. A smooth irreducible representation of depth zero is said to contain a Moy-Prasad type
(x,\mathcal{X})\in\mathrm{MP}(0)
if the restriction
\mathrm{res}_{G_{x,0}}(\pi)
of
\pi

to the parahoric
G_{x,0}
contains
\mathcal{X}
. From
[
MP94
, Theorem 5.2]
, we know that any smooth irreducible depth-zero representation
\pi
contains a depth-zero Moy-Prasad type, and any two Moy-Prasad types contained
\pi
are associates of each other.

We recall briefly how to attach Deligne-Lusztig parameters to depth-zero Moy-Prasad types, following
[
CB24
, Section 5.4]
and
[
CDT25
, Lemma 59]
. Let
(x,\mathcal{X})
be a depth-zero Moy-Prasad type, and let
S
be a
k
-split maximal torus such that
x\in\mathcal{A}_{S}
. Then,
\mathcal{X}
is the inflation of a irreducible cuspidal representation of
\mathbb{G}_{x}^{F}
. Using methods in
[
Lus07
, Section 16]
and identifying
X^{*}(S)
and
X^{*}(\mathbb{S})
, we can attach to
\mathcal{X}
and element
\theta_{S,\mathcal{X}}\in(S^{\vee}//W_{x,S})^{[q]}
where all the notations denote the ususal objects as defined earlier in this article and in
[
CDT25
]
. Let
\theta_{x,\mathcal{X}}
denote the image of
\theta_{S,\mathcal{X}}
along the map
(S^{\vee}//W_{x,S})^{[q]}\rightarrow(S^{\vee}//W_{S})^{[q]}\rightarrow\mathrm{DL}_{0}
and
(x,\mathcal{X})\mapsto\theta_{x,\mathcal{X}}
gives us the desired map
\mathrm{MP}(0)\rightarrow\mathrm{DL}_{0}
. This is independent of the choice of apartment containing
x
, since all such apartments are
G_{x,0}
-conjugates. Equivalently, pick
g\in G(k)
such that
gx\in\mathscr{A}
. Then the image of
\theta_{T,\prescript{g}{}{}\mathcal{X}}\in(T^{\vee}//W_{gx})^{[q]}
along
(T^{\vee}//W_{gx})^{[q]}\rightarrow(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}
gives the representative of the corresponding Deligne-Lusztig parameter in
(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}
in accordance with the definition in
[
CB24
]
. The fact that this is independent of the choice of
g
can be shown similarly to
[
CDT25
, Lemma 23]
. So, without loss of generality we can denote it by
\theta_{x,\mathcal{X}}
as well, and we note that
\theta_{x,\mathcal{X}}=\theta_{gx,\prescript{g}{}{}\mathcal{X}}
.

Proposition 7.11
.

Let
(\pi,V)
be a smooth irreducible depth-zero representation and let
(x,\mathcal{X})
be a depth-zero Moy-Prasad type contained in
(\pi,V)
. Then,
\theta_{x,\mathcal{X}}=\Theta_{0}(\pi)
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we can assume
x\in\bar{\mathcal{C}}
. There exists
\sigma\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}]
with
x\in\sigma
such that
G_{x,0+}=G_{\sigma,0+}
. Pick
v\in V^{G_{\sigma,0+}}
such that
\pi^{G_{\sigma,0+}}(g)v=\mathcal{X}(g)v
for
g\in\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}
. For
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}]
, we have

\lambda(\Theta_{0}(\pi))v=\xi^{0}(\lambda)v=\sum_{g\in\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}}i_{\sigma,0}(\lambda)(g)\pi^{G_{\sigma,0+}}(g)v=\sum_{g\in\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}}i_{\sigma,0}(\lambda)(g)\mathcal{X}(g)v

Using (
7.1.2
) and the definition of
i_{\sigma,0}
, we have

\sum_{g\in\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}}i_{\sigma,0}(\lambda)(g)\mathcal{X}(g)v=\sum_{g\in\mathbb{G}_{\sigma}^{F}}\rho_{\sigma,o}\circ t^{*}_{\sigma,0}(\lambda)(g)\mathcal{X}(g)v=t^{*}_{\sigma,0}(\lambda)(\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}(\mathcal{X}))v=\lambda(\theta_{x,\mathcal{X}})v

where the last step follows from the definition of
\theta_{x,\mathcal{X}}
in the previous paragraph. Thus
\lambda(\Theta_{0}(\pi))=\lambda(\theta_{x,\mathcal{X}})
for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}]
, and hence
\Theta_{0}(\pi)=\theta_{x,\mathcal{X}}
.
∎

Remark 7.12
.

This gives an alternative proof of the fact that any two depth-zero Moy-Prasad types contained a smooth irreducible representation of depth zero have the same Deligne-Lusztig parameter attached to it.

7.5
Restricted depth-zero parameters

Let
\mathfrak{F}
denote a (geometric) Frobenius in
W_{k}
, and we denote its image in
W_{k}/I^{0+}_{k}
by
\mathfrak{F}
as well.

Definition 7.13
.

A depth-zero Langlands parameter is a continuous cocycle

\phi:W_{k}/I^{0+}_{k}\rightarrow G^{\vee}

such that
\phi(\mathfrak{F})
is semi-simple. A restricted depth-zero parameter is a continuous cocycle

\varphi:I_{k}/I^{0+}_{k}\rightarrow G^{\vee}

which is a restriction from a depth-zero Langlands parameter. We denote by
\mathrm{RP}_{0}
the set of
G^{\vee}
conjugacy classes of restricted depth-zero parameters.

If we fix a Borel subgroup
T\subset B
, we get a based root datum of
G

\psi_{0}(G)=(X^{*}(T),\Delta,X_{*}(T),\Delta^{\vee})
where
\Delta
is the set of positive simple roots determined by
B
. We fix a pinning
(G^{\vee},B^{\vee},T^{\vee},e^{\vee}=\{x_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in\Delta}
, and the action of
W_{k}
on
\psi_{0}(G)
induces a
W_{k}
-action on
G^{\vee}
denoted by
\mu_{G}:W_{k}\rightarrow\mathrm{Aut}((G^{\vee},B^{\vee},T^{\vee},e^{\vee}=\{x_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in\Delta})\cong\mathrm{Aut}(\psi_{0}(G))
. If
G
splits over a tamely ramified extension, the action
\mu_{G}
of factors through
W_{k}/I^{0+}_{k}
and hence we did not have to consider cocycles for restricted postive depth parameters. The set
\mathrm{RP}_{0}
is independent of the choice of pinning since all pinnings are
G^{\vee}
-conjugate. Since we have assumed
G
to be split over
k
, we immediately see that the action is trivial in our case and the depth-zero parameters (restricted or otherwise) are just homomprphisms
I_{k}/I^{0+}_{k}\rightarrow G^{\vee}
and
W_{k}/I^{0+}_{k}\rightarrow G^{\vee}
with required properties as mentioned in the definition.

As per
[
CDT25
, Lemma 62]
, there is a bijections
\mathrm{RP}_{0}\cong\mathrm{DL}_{0}
and we get a map

\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{0}\xrightarrow{\Theta_{0}}\mathrm{DL}_{0}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{RP}_{0}

Further, the bijection
\mathrm{RP}_{0}\cong\mathrm{DL}_{0}
depends on the choice of an isomorphism
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}\simeq(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})_{p^{\prime}}
, and making the same choice as in
7.1.1
makes the composed map
\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{0}\rightarrow\mathrm{RP}_{0}
independent of the choice.

8
Decomposing the category of smooth representations

Definition 8.1
.

Let
\{\mathscr{C}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a family of full subcategories of
R(G)
. We can decompose
R(G)
as a product of full subcategories and write

R(G)=\prod_{i\in I}\mathscr{C}_{i}

if every
(\pi,V)
decomposes as
V=\oplus_{i\in I}V_{i}
with
V_{i}\in\mathscr{C}_{i}
and for any
V_{i}\in\mathscr{C}_{i}
,
V_{j}\in\mathscr{C}_{j}
, we have
\mathrm{Hom}_{G(k)}(V_{i},V_{j})=0
if
i\neq j
.

For
(\pi,V)\in R(G)
, let
\mathrm{JH}(\pi)
(or
\mathrm{JH}(V)
) denote the set of (isomorphism classes of ) irreducible subquotients of
\pi
(also called Jordan-Hölder factors of
\pi
). Let
\mathrm{Irr}(G)=\coprod_{\alpha\in A}S_{\alpha}
be a partition of the set of (isomorphism classes of) smooth irreducible representations of
G(k)
. For
S_{\alpha}\subset\mathrm{Irr}(G)
, let
R(G)_{S_{\alpha}}
denote the full subcategory of
R(G)
defined as
R(G)_{S_{\alpha}}:=\{(\pi,V)\in R(G)\>|\>\mathrm{JH}(\pi)\subseteq S_{\alpha}\}
. The subcategory
R(G)_{S_{\alpha}}
is closed under the formation of subquotients, extensions and direct sums. For
(\pi,V)\in R(G)
, let
V_{S_{\alpha}}
denote the sum of all
G(k)
-invariant subspaces of
V
which lie in
R(G)_{S_{\alpha}}
. It is the unique maximal
G(k)
-subspace of
V
in
R(G)_{S_{\alpha}}
. We immediately observe that
V_{S_{\alpha}}\cap V_{S_{\alpha}^{\prime}}=\{0\}
and
\mathrm{Hom}_{G(k)}(V_{S_{\alpha}},V_{S_{\alpha}^{\prime}})=\{0\}
. For detailed proofs, check
[
Hey23
, Section 16]
.

Definition 8.2
.

We say that
\{S_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in A}
splits
(\pi,V)\in R(G)
if
V
can be written as a direct sum

V=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in A}V_{S_{\alpha}}

We say
\{S_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in A}
splits
R(G)
if it splits every
(\pi,V)\in R(G)
, i.e., if
R(G)
decomposes into a product of full subcategories

R(G)=\prod_{\alpha\in A}R(G)_{S_{\alpha}}.

As mentioned in
[
CDT25
, Remark 30]
, if
p
does not divide the order of the absolute Weyl group of
G
, then
[
Fin21
, Theorem 6.1]
implies that every non-degenerate positive depth Moy-Prasad type has depth
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
. We henceforth impose that condition on the characteristic of the residue field, and hence for any
\pi\in\mathrm{Irr}(G)
,
\rho(\pi)\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}
.

For
p\nmid|W|
, (
7.3.1
), gives a map

\Theta:\mathrm{Irr}(G)=\coprod_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}}\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{r}\longrightarrow\mathrm{DL}_{0}\coprod\left(\coprod_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{>0}}\mathrm{DL}_{r}^{\circ}\right)=:\mathrm{DL}^{t}

(8.0.1)

and hence we can partition the set of smooth irreducible representations of
G(k)
into packets using the Deligne-Lusztig parameters. Let
\vartheta^{r}\in\mathrm{DL}_{r}
for
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}
and
\Pi(\vartheta^{r})=\{\pi\in\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{r}\>|\>\Theta_{r}(\pi)=\vartheta^{r}\}
. We have a partition

\mathrm{Irr}(G)=\coprod_{\vartheta^{0}\in\mathrm{DL}_{0}}\Pi(\vartheta^{0})\coprod\left(\coprod_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}}\coprod_{\vartheta^{r}\in\mathrm{DL}^{\circ}_{r}}\Pi(\vartheta^{r})\right)=\coprod_{\vartheta\in\mathrm{DL}^{t}}\Pi(\vartheta)

(8.0.2)

For
\vartheta\in\mathrm{DL}^{t}
, let
R(G)_{\vartheta}
denote the full subcategory of
R(G)
such that
R(G)_{\vartheta}=\{(\pi,V)\in R(G)\>|\>\mathrm{JH}(\pi)\subseteq\Pi(\vartheta)\}
(instead of
R(G)_{\Pi(\vartheta)}
, to simplify notation). The aim of this section is to prove the following theorem.

Theorem 8.3
.

We have a decomposition of
R(G)
as a product of full subcategories

R(G)=\prod_{\vartheta\in\mathrm{DL}^{t}}R(G)_{\vartheta}

In concrete terms, we want to show that for each
(\pi,V)\in R(G)
, we have
G(k)
-invariant subspaces
V_{\vartheta}\in R(G)_{\vartheta}
such that
V=\oplus_{\vartheta\in\mathrm{DL}^{t}}V_{\vartheta}
(i.e., the partition in (
8.0.2
) splits
R(G)
). We show this by producing projectors to
R(G)_{\vartheta}
in the Bernstein center for each
\vartheta\in\mathrm{DL}^{t}
, and proving a finiteness condition.

Let
X^{*}(G)=\mathrm{Hom}_{k}(G,\mathbb{G}_{m})
denote the group of
k
-rational characters of
G
. For
\chi\in X^{*}(G)
, considering it as a group homomorphism
G(k)\rightarrow k^{\times}
,
\mathrm{v}(\chi(g))\in\mathbb{Z}
for
g\in G(k)
. Let
G(k)^{0}
denote the subgroup

G(k)^{0}=\{g\in G(k)\>|\>\mathrm{v}(\chi(g))=0\>\forall\>\chi\in X^{*}(G)\}

The subgroup
G(k)^{0}
is open, normal and contains all compact subgroups of
G(k)
. A character
\chi:G(k)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
is called unramified if
G(k)^{0}
lies in its kernel. We denote the group of unramified characters of
G(k)
by
X^{ur}(G)
and it can be identified with
\mathrm{Hom}(G(k)/G(k)^{0},\mathbb{C}^{\times})
.

Definition 8.4
.

We define a cuspidal pair (or cuspidal datum) of
G
to be a pair
(L,\varrho)
where
L
is a
k
-Levi subgroup of
G
and
\varrho
is an irreducible supercuspidal representation of
L(k)
.

Two such pairs
(L,\varrho)
and
(L^{\prime},\varrho^{\prime})
are called associated if there exists
g\in G(k)
such that
gLg^{-1}=L^{\prime}
and the map
\mathrm{Ad}(g):L(k)\xrightarrow{\simeq}L^{\prime}(k)
induces an isomorphism
\prescript{g}{}{}\varrho\cong\varrho^{\prime}
. We denote the
G(k)
-conjugacy class of
(L,\varrho)
by
(L,\varrho)_{G}
, and let
\Omega(G)
denote the set of equivalence classes of cuspidal pairs modulo association. From
[
Roc09
, Proposition 1.7.2.1]
, we see that for each
\pi\in\mathrm{Irr}(G)
, there exists a unique
(L,\varrho)_{G}\in\Omega(G)
such that
\pi
is isomorphic to a subquotient of
i^{G}_{P}(\varrho)
, where
P
is a
k
-parabolic subgroup of
G
with Levi component
L
and
i^{G}_{P}
denotes the normalized parabolic induction functor
i^{G}_{P}:R(L)\rightarrow R(G)
. Thus, the assignment
\pi\mapsto(L,\varrho)_{G}
gives us a well defined surjective map
\mathrm{CS}:\mathrm{Irr}(G)\rightarrow\Omega(G)
, and
\mathrm{CS}(\pi)=(L,\varrho)_{G}
is called the cuspidal support of
\pi
.

We define another equivalence relation called inertial equivalence on the set of cuspidal data of
G
in the following way:
(L,\varrho)\sim(L^{\prime},\varrho^{\prime})
if there exists
g\in G(k)
and
\omega\in X^{ur}(L^{\prime})
such that
\prescript{g}{}{}L=L^{\prime}
,
\prescript{g}{}{}\varrho\cong\varrho^{\prime}\otimes\omega
. We denote the inertial equivalence class of
(L,\varrho)
by
[L,\varrho]_{G}
. This is a coarser relation than association, and we denote the set of equivalence classes of cuspidal pairs modulo inertial equivalence by
\mathfrak{B}(G)
. We denote by
\mathrm{IS}
the composition

\mathrm{IS}:\mathrm{Irr}(G)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{CS}}\Omega(G){\mathrel{\to}^{\Upsilon}}\mathfrak{B}(G)

and
\mathrm{IS}(\pi)=[L,\varrho]_{G}
is called the inertial support of
\pi
, where
\pi
is an irreducible subquotient of
i^{G}_{P^{\prime}}(\varrho\otimes\omega)
for some
k
-parabolic
P^{\prime}
with Levi component
L
and
\omega\in X^{ur}(L)
. In fact, it is enough to take
P^{\prime}=P
(check
[
Roc09
, Corollary 1.10.4.3]
).

For
\mathfrak{a}\in\mathfrak{B}(G)
, let
\Omega(G)_{\mathfrak{a}}=\Upsilon^{-1}(\mathfrak{a})
and we have
\Omega(G)=\coprod_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathfrak{B}(G)}\Omega(G)_{\mathfrak{a}}
. The set
\Omega(G)
has a natural structure of a complex algebraic variety, with connected components given by
\Omega(G)_{\mathfrak{a}}
(check
[
Hai14
, Section 3.3]
). We have an isomorphism
\mathcal{Z}(G)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathbb{C}[\Omega(G)]
between the ring of regular functions on this variety and
\mathcal{Z}(G)
.

The set of inertial equivalence classes gives a partition of the
\mathrm{Irr}(G)
given by

\mathrm{Irr}(G)=\coprod_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathfrak{B}(G)}\mathrm{IS}^{-1}(\mathfrak{a})

and we define
R(G)_{\mathfrak{a}}:=\{(\pi,V)\in R(G)\>|\>\mathrm{JH}(\pi)\subseteq\mathrm{IS}^{-1}(\mathfrak{a})\}
. This is an indecomposable full subcategory of
R(G)
, and we have the Bernstein decomposition theorem

Theorem 8.5

(Theorem 1.7.3.1 in
[
Roc09
]
)
.

We have a decomposition of
R(G)
as a product of indecomposable full subcategories

R(G)=\prod_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathfrak{B}(G)}R(G)_{\mathfrak{a}}

We fix a choice of
\nu_{m}\in k^{t}
such that
\nu_{m}
lies in a finite tamely ramified extension of
k
and
\mathrm{v}(\nu_{1/m})=1/m
for
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and
m\nmid p
. For
r=i/m\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
, define
\nu_{r}=(\nu_{1/m})^{i}
. Further, we fix an isomorphism
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times}\simeq(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})_{p^{\prime}}
. With these choices and the choice of a fixed
k
-split maximal torus
T
of
G
, we have fixed the maps
\xi^{r}
as defined in Theorem
7.7
(for
r=0)
and Theorem
5.7
(for
r>0
) for each
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}
. Using the maps
\xi^{r}
, we will construct projectors to
R(G)_{\vartheta}
for
\vartheta\in\mathrm{DL}^{t}
, and use the Bernstein decomposition theorem to give a finiteness condition, thereby proving Theorem
8.3
.

Since we have fixed
\nu_{r}
for each
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
, we can represent
\vartheta^{r}\in\mathrm{DL}_{r}
by the pair
(\nu_{r},\theta_{\nu_{r}})
,
\theta_{\nu_{r}}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}
in the equivalence class of
\vartheta^{r}
. Similarly, for any
\pi\in\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{r}
,
\Theta(\pi)
can be represented by the pair
(\nu_{r},\tilde{\theta}_{\nu_{r}}(\pi))
, as defined in Section
6.1
. With the fixed maximal torus
T
,
\mathrm{DL}_{0}
can be identified with the set
(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}
.

Proposition 8.6
.

For
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
, let
\vartheta^{r}=\overline{(\nu_{r},\theta_{\nu_{r}})}\in\mathrm{DL}_{r}^{\circ}
and
\mathbbm{1}_{\theta_{\nu_{r}}}\in\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]
denote the characteristic function of
\theta_{\nu_{r}}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}\setminus\{\bar{0}\}
. Then,
e_{\vartheta^{r}}=\xi^{r}(\mathbbm{1}_{\theta_{\nu_{r}}})
is an idempotent in
\mathcal{Z}^{r}(G)
and acts as the projector to
R(G)_{\vartheta^{r}}
, i.e., for
(\pi,V)\in\mathrm{Irr}(G)
,

e_{\vartheta^{r}}|_{V}=\begin{cases}\mathrm{Id}_{V},&\text{ if }\rho(\pi)=r\text{ and }\Theta(\pi)=\vartheta^{r}\\
0,&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}

For
r=0
, let
\vartheta^{0}\in(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}\equiv\mathrm{DL}_{0}
and let
\mathbbm{1}_{\vartheta^{0}}\in\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}]
similarly denote the characteristic function of
\vartheta^{0}\in(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}
. Then,
e_{\vartheta^{0}}=\xi^{r}(\mathbbm{1}_{\vartheta^{0}})
is an idempotent in
\mathcal{Z}^{0}(G)
and acts as the projector to
R(G)_{\vartheta^{0}}
.

Proof.

Let us first consider the case of
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
, and let
r\in\frac{1}{m}\mathbb{Z}
. Note that we only need to consider
\vartheta^{r}\in\mathrm{DL}^{\circ}_{r}
because
