\Theta(\pi)\in\mathrm{DL}^{\circ}_{r}
for
\pi\in\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{r}
, as stated in Remark
6.6
.

Let
(\pi,V)\in\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{<r}
. Then, there exists
\sigma\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{m}]
such that
V^{G_{\sigma,r}}\ni v\neq 0
. We have

e_{\vartheta^{r}}(v)=\xi^{r}(\mathbbm{1}_{\theta_{\nu_{r}}})v=\mathbbm{1}_{\theta_{\nu_{r}}}(\bar{0})v

where the last step follows from Theorem
5.0.1
. Since
\theta_{\nu_{r}}\neq\bar{0}
, we have
e_{\vartheta^{r}}(v)=0
and hence
e_{\vartheta^{r}}|_{V}=0
for
(\pi,V)\in\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{<r}
. Since the image of
\xi^{r}
lies in
\mathcal{Z}^{r}(G)
, it immediately follows that
e_{\vartheta^{r}}|_{V}=0
for
(\pi,V)\in\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{>r}
.

For
(\pi,V)\in\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{r}
, let
\sigma\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{m}]
be such that
\exists\>0\neq v\in V^{G_{\sigma,r+}}
. If
(\pi,V)\in\Pi(\vartheta^{r})\subset\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{r}
, then
\Theta(\pi)=\overline{(\nu_{r},\tilde{\theta}_{\nu_{r}}(\pi))}
with
\tilde{\theta}_{\nu_{r}}(\pi)=\theta_{\nu_{r}}
and

e_{\vartheta^{r}}(v)=\xi^{r}(\mathbbm{1}_{\theta_{\nu_{r}}})v=\mathbbm{1}_{\theta_{\nu_{r}}}(\tilde{\theta}_{\nu_{r}}(\pi))v=v

which shows that
e_{\vartheta^{r}}|_{V}=\mathrm{Id}_{V}
for
(\pi,V)\in\Pi(\vartheta^{r})
. If
(\pi,V)\in\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{r}\setminus\Pi(\vartheta^{r})
, then
\tilde{\theta}_{\nu_{r}}(\pi)\neq\theta_{\nu_{r}}
and we have

e_{\vartheta^{r}}(v)=\xi^{r}(\mathbbm{1}_{\theta_{\nu_{r}}})v=\mathbbm{1}_{\theta_{\nu_{r}}}(\tilde{\theta}_{\nu_{r}}(\pi))v=0.

The fact that
e_{\vartheta^{r}}
is an idempotent in
\mathcal{Z}^{r}(G)
immediately follows since
\xi^{r}:\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}^{r}(G)
is an algebra morphism. This finishes the case of
r>0
.

For
(\pi,V)\in\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{0}
, let
\sigma\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}]
be such that
\exists\>0\neq v\in V^{G_{\sigma,0+}}
. Let
\vartheta^{0}\in\mathrm{DL}_{0}
. If
\pi\in\Pi(\vartheta^{0})
, then
\Theta(\pi)=\vartheta^{0}
and we have

e_{\vartheta^{0}}(v)=\xi^{0}(\mathbbm{1}_{\vartheta^{0}})v=\mathbbm{1}_{\vartheta^{0}}(\Theta(\pi))v=v

which shows
e_{\vartheta^{0}}|_{V}=\mathrm{Id}_{V}
for
(\pi,V)\in\Pi(\vartheta^{0})
. If
(\pi,V)\in\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{0}\setminus\Pi(\vartheta^{0})
, then
\Theta(\pi)\neq\vartheta^{0}
and

e_{\vartheta^{0}}(v)=\xi^{0}(\mathbbm{1}_{\vartheta^{0}})v=\mathbbm{1}_{\vartheta^{0}}(\Theta(\pi))v=0

The element
e_{\vartheta^{0}}(v)\in\mathcal{Z}^{0}(G)
is an idempotent since
\xi^{0}
is an algebra map. Thus, for
(\pi,V)\in\mathrm{Irr}(G)
and
\vartheta\in\mathrm{DL}^{t}

e_{\vartheta}|_{V}=\begin{cases}\mathrm{Id}_{V},&\text{ if }\Theta(\pi)=\vartheta\\
0,&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}

which finishes the proof.
∎

Proposition 8.7
.

For
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
, let
\bar{0}^{r}=\overline{(\nu_{r},\bar{0})}\in\mathrm{DL}_{r}
be the trivial Deligne-Lusztig parameter at depth-
r
and
\mathbbm{1}_{\bar{0}}\in\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]
denote the characteristic function of
\bar{0}\in(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}
. Then,
e_{\bar{0}^{r}}=\xi^{r}(\mathbbm{1}_{\bar{0}})
is an idempotent in
\mathcal{Z}^{r}(G)
and acts as the projector to
R(G)_{<r}
, i.e., for
(\pi,V)\in\mathrm{Irr}(G)
,

e_{\bar{0}^{r}}|_{V}=\begin{cases}\mathrm{Id}_{V},&\text{ if }\rho(\pi)<r\\
0,&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}

Proof.

Let
(\pi,V)\in\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{<r}
for
r\in\frac{1}{m}\mathbb{Z}
. Then, there exists
\sigma\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{m}]
such that
V^{G_{\sigma,r}}\ni v\neq 0
. We have

e_{\bar{0}^{r}}(v)=\xi^{r}(\mathbbm{1}_{\bar{0}})v=\mathbbm{1}_{\bar{0}}(\bar{0})v=v

where the last step follows from Theorem
5.0.1
. Since the image of
\xi^{r}
lies in
\mathcal{Z}^{r}(G)
, it immediately follows that
e_{\vartheta^{r}}|_{V}=0
for
(\pi,V)\in\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{>r}
.

For
(\pi,V)\in\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{r}
, let
\sigma\in[\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{m}]
be such that
\exists\>0\neq v\in V^{G_{\sigma,r+}}
. Then
\Theta(\pi)=\overline{(\nu_{r},\tilde{\theta}_{\nu_{r}}(\pi))}\neq\bar{0}^{r}
with
\tilde{\theta}_{\nu_{r}}(\pi)\neq\bar{0}
, and

e_{\bar{0}^{r}}(v)=\xi^{r}(\mathbbm{1}_{\bar{0}})v=\mathbbm{1}_{\bar{0}}(\tilde{\theta}_{\nu_{r}}(\pi))v=0

which shows that
e_{\bar{0}^{r}}|_{V}=0
for
(\pi,V)\in\mathrm{Irr}(G)
.
∎

Now, we prove the main theorem of the section.

Proof of Theorem
8.3
.

Let
(\pi,V)\in R(G)
and
\vartheta\in\mathrm{DL}^{t}
. Since
e_{\vartheta}\in\mathcal{Z}(G)
is an idempotent, it is immediate that it projects onto a
G(k)
-invariant subspace of
V
, and we define it to be
V_{\vartheta}:=e_{\vartheta}(V)
. From Proposition
8.6
, we see that
V_{\vartheta}\in R(G)_{\vartheta}
and it is the unique maximal
G(k)
-subspace of
V\in R(G)_{\vartheta}
. It is immediate that
V_{\vartheta}\cap V_{\vartheta^{\prime}}=\{0\}
for
\vartheta\neq\vartheta^{\prime}\in\mathrm{DL}^{t}
and hence
V_{\vartheta}\oplus V_{\vartheta^{\prime}}\subseteq V
, and
\mathrm{Hom}_{G(k)}(V_{1},V_{1}^{\prime})=0
if
V_{1}\in R(G)_{\vartheta}
,
V_{1}^{\prime}\in R(G)_{\vartheta^{\prime}}
. Note that this is enough to decompose representations of finite length, i.e., we have proved that
\{\Pi(\vartheta)\}_{\vartheta\in\mathrm{DL}^{t}}
splits any representation of finite length. To extend this to all smooth representations, we need a finiteness condition.

Using Theorem
8.5
, we know that we can write
V
as a direct sum

V=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathfrak{B}(G)}V_{\mathfrak{a}}

where
V_{\mathfrak{a}}
is the unique maximal
G(k)
-subspace of
V
in
R(G)_{\mathfrak{a}}
. Then, the set

\overline{\mathrm{IS}}(\pi)=\{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathfrak{B}(G)\>|\>V_{\mathfrak{a}}\neq 0\}

is a finite set. For a
k
-Levi subgroup
L
of
G
, we denote the depth of
\varrho\in\mathrm{Irr}(L)
by
\rho_{L}(\varrho)
. If
\varsigma\in\mathrm{Irr}(G)
is an irreducible subquotient of
V_{\mathfrak{a}}
for
\mathfrak{a}=[L,\varrho]_{G}
, then we know that
\varsigma
is isomorphic to a subquotient of
i_{P}^{G}(\varrho\otimes\omega)
for some
\omega\in X^{ur}(L)
and
P
a
k
-parabolic subgroup of
G
with Levi component
L
. Since
L(k)^{0}
contains all compact subgroups of
L(k)
,
\rho_{L}(\varrho)=\rho_{L}(\sigma\otimes\omega)
for any
\omega\in X^{ur}(L)
. From
[
MP96
, Theorem 5.2]
, we know that depth is preserved under parabolic induction and hence
\rho(\varsigma)=\rho_{L}(\varrho)
. Since
\overline{\mathrm{IS}}(\pi)
is finite, the set

\bar{\rho}(\pi)=\{\rho(\varrho)\>|\>\mathfrak{a}=[L,\varrho]_{G}\in\overline{\mathrm{IS}}(\pi)\}

is a finite set consisting of elements in
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}
. If
\vartheta^{r}\in\mathrm{DL}_{r}
for
r\not\in\bar{\rho}(\pi)
, we can immediately observe that
V_{\vartheta^{r}}=e_{\vartheta^{r}}(V)=0
, and hence we only need to consider finite number of depths. Further note that
\mathrm{DL}_{r}
is finite for
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}
. This gives the necessary finiteness condition and we see that

V=\bigoplus_{r\in\bar{\rho}(\pi)}\bigoplus_{\vartheta^{r}\in\mathrm{DL}_{r}}V_{\vartheta^{r}}

Since
(\pi,V)\in R(G)
was arbitrary, we have that any
(\pi,V)\in R(G)
can be written as a direct sum

V=\bigoplus_{\vartheta\in\mathrm{DL}^{t}}V_{\vartheta}

which finshes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Consider the idempotent elements
e_{r}:=\sum_{\vartheta^{r}\in\mathrm{DL}_{r}^{\circ}}e_{\vartheta^{r}}
for
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
and
e_{0}=\sum_{\vartheta^{0}\in\mathrm{DL}_{0}}e_{\vartheta^{0}}=[A_{\delta_{0}}]
. Then, for
(\pi,V)\in R(G)
,
e_{r}(V)\subset V
is a subrepresentation which has irreducible subquotients of only depth
r
. Further, the projector
e_{\bar{0}^{r}}
constructed in Proposition
8.7
projects onto the depth
<r
part. Let
R(G)_{r}=\{(\pi,V)\in R(G)\>|\>\mathrm{JH}(\pi)\subset\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{r}\}
and
R(G)_{<r}
be defined similarly. Then we have a decomposition
R(G)_{\leq r}=R(G)_{r}\oplus R(G)_{<r}
via the projectors that we defined. Let us define
R(G)_{\bar{0}^{r}}:=R(G)_{<r}
consistent with the notation
R(G)_{\vartheta^{r}}
for
\vartheta^{r}\in\mathrm{DL}_{r}^{0}
with the corresponding projector
e_{\vartheta^{r}}
. Further, using the bijection
\mathrm{DL}_{r}\rightarrow\mathrm{RP}_{r}
, we can parametrize these subcategories by
\varphi^{r}\in\mathrm{RP}_{r}
. Let
\tilde{\Theta}_{r}
denotes the composition
\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{r}\xrightarrow{\Theta}\mathrm{DL}_{r}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{RP}_{r}
for
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}
,
\mathrm{RP}_{r}^{\circ}
denote the non-trivial restricted Langlands parameters for positive depth and
\mathrm{RP}^{t}
be defined similarly to
\mathrm{DL}^{t}
in (
8.0.1
). Then, we have a map
\tilde{\Theta}:\mathrm{Irr}(G)\rightarrow\mathrm{RP}^{t}
and we can equivalently denote
R(G)_{\vartheta},\>\vartheta\in\mathrm{DL}^{t}
by
R(G)_{\varphi},\>\varphi\in\mathrm{RP}^{t}
if
\vartheta\mapsto\varphi
via the bijection
\mathrm{DL}_{r}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{RP}_{r}
described in
[
CDT25
]
. Then we have the following decompositions as a corollary of Theorem
8.3
and the previous propositions:

Corollary 8.8
.

We have a decomposition of
R(G)
as a product of full subcategories

R(G)=\prod_{\varphi\in\mathrm{RP}^{t}}R(G)_{\varphi}

Further, we have a decomposition for
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{>0}

R(G)_{r}=\bigoplus_{\varphi^{r}\in\mathrm{RP}_{r}^{\circ}}R(G)_{\varphi^{r}}=\bigoplus_{\vartheta_{r}\in\mathrm{DL}_{r}^{\circ}}R(G)_{\vartheta^{r}}

and the following decomposition for
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}

R(G)_{\leq r}=\bigoplus_{\varphi^{r}\in\mathrm{RP}_{r}}R(G)_{\varphi^{r}}=\bigoplus_{\vartheta_{r}\in\mathrm{DL}_{r}}R(G)_{\vartheta^{r}}

where the subcategory corresponding to the trivial depth-
r
parameter for positive depths contains the representations with all their irreducible subquotients in
\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{<r}
.

9
Some conjectures and comments

Most of the statements in this section are conjectural. Based on the results that we have proved and some existing conjectures about Langlands parameters and stable center, we make some predictions about the elements in
\mathcal{Z}^{r}(G)
that we constructed as the images of the maps in Theorems
5.7
and
7.7
.

We assume the characteristic of the residue field of
k
is sufficiently large, atleast
p\nmid|W|
. Let
\phi:WD_{k}=W_{k}\ltimes\mathbb{C}\rightarrow\prescript{L}{}{}G
be a Langlands parameter for
G(k)
. We have a notion of depth of a Langlands parameter, which we denote by
\rho(\phi)
.

\rho(\phi)=\text{min}\{r\in\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}\>|\>\phi|_{I_{k}^{r+}}\text{ is trivial }\}

Let
\Pi(\phi)
denote the
L
-packet corresponding to
\phi
and
\pi\in\Pi(\phi)
. The relation between
\rho(\pi)
and
\rho(\phi)
has been studied quite a bit, and under some assumptions (like the
G
being split over tamely ramified extension and some conditions on the residue field), it has been conjectured that depth is preserved, i.e.,
\rho(\pi)=\rho(\phi)
for any
\pi\in\Pi(\phi)
(check
[
CDT25
, Conjecture 52]
). Depth-preservation for local Langlands under certain conditions is known in several cases like the tamely ramified tori (
[
Yu09a
, Section 7.10]
), unitary groups (
[
Oi23
]
,
[
Oi21
]
),
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
(
[
Gan15
]
) and inner forms of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
(
[
ABPS16
]
).

Assuming conjectural depth preservation basically implies that the restriction
\phi|_{W_{k}}
of a Langlands parameter
\phi_{\pi}
of
\pi\in\mathrm{Irr}(G)
to
W_{k}
factors through
\phi_{\pi}|_{W_{k}}:W_{k}/I_{k}^{\rho(\pi)+}\rightarrow G^{\vee}
. Further, the conjectures 47 and 65 (and some other conjectures which follow from them in Section 6) from
[
CDT25
]
suggest that the restriction of a Langlands parameter
\phi_{\pi}
of
\pi
to
I_{k}^{\rho(\pi)}
is
G^{\vee}
conjugate to the restricted Langlands parameter attached to if via the map
\mathrm{Irr}(G)_{r}\rightarrow\mathrm{DL}_{r}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{RP}_{r}
for
\rho(\pi)=r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}
described in this work and
[
CDT25
]
. These conjectures suggest that the packets
\Pi(\vartheta)
for
\vartheta\in\mathrm{DL}^{t}
that we constructed in the previous section are unions of
L
-packets conjectured by the Local Langlands correspondence. In fact, they are actually unions of infinitesimal classes as descibed in
2.3
.

We fix a choice of
\nu_{m}\in k^{t}
and in isomorphism
\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{\times}\simeq(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})_{p^{\prime}}
as in Section
8
, which fixes the maps
\xi^{r}
as described in Theorems
7.7
and
5.7
for
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}
, and consider the elements in
\mathcal{Z}(G)
which are in the image of
\xi^{r}
. If
z\in\mathrm{Im}(\xi^{r})
and
\pi_{1},\pi_{2}\in\mathrm{Irr}(G)
such that
\Theta(\pi_{1})=\Theta(\pi_{2})
, then
f_{z}(\pi_{1})=f_{z}(\pi_{2})
with
f_{z}
as defined in
2.3
. In particular,
z\in\mathcal{Z}(G)
acts by the same constant on all irreducible representations contained in an infinitesimal class, since they are contained in
\Pi(\vartheta)
for some
\vartheta\in\mathrm{DL}^{t}
. Note that this is much coarser. Hence, the conjectural description of the stable center
\mathcal{Z}^{st}(G)
suggests the following conjecture:

Conjecture 9.1
.

Let
\mathcal{Z}^{st,r}(G)=\mathcal{Z}^{st}(G)\cap\mathcal{Z}^{r}(G)
for
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\cap\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}
and
\xi^{r}
be the maps described in Theorems
5.7
and
7.7
. Then,
\mathrm{Im}(\xi^{r})\subset\mathcal{Z}^{st,r}(G)
.

By slight abuse of notation, we denote by
\mathbbm{1}
the complex function in both
\mathbb{C}[(\bar{\mathfrak{t}}^{*}//W)^{F}]
and
\mathbb{C}[(T^{\vee}//W)^{[q]}]
which takes the value
1
at all points. Then, the idempotent element
\xi^{r}(\mathbbm{1})\in\mathcal{Z}^{r}(G)
is exactly the depth-
r
projector
[A_{\delta_{r}}]
as described in
[
BKV15
]
. The depth-
r
projector was shown to be stable in
[
BKV15
]
. A geometric approach was used in
[
BKV13
]
to show stability of the depth-zero projector. These provide some further evidence and possible approaches to prove the conjecture. Note that we would only need to prove that the projectors
e_{\vartheta}
for
\vartheta\in\mathrm{DL}^{t}
as described Proposition
8.6
are stable.
